{\mathsf{D}}_{A}(\mathcal{O})\to{\mathsf{D}}_{A}(\mathcal{O})
.

13.1.
Manifolds and complexes

Consider the algebraic group
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
over
F
. Let
K_{\infty}\subseteq\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(F\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}{\mathbb{R}})
be a maximal compact subgroup. For any compact open subgroup
K=\prod_{v}K_{v}\subseteq\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\infty})
consider the topological space:

Y_{K}=\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(F)\backslash\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/KK_{\infty}

which is an orbifold of dimension
2r_{1}+3r_{2}
. We say that
K
is
sufficiently small
if
gKg^{-1}\cap\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(F)
is torsion-free for all
g\in\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\infty})
.

We note the following lemma (
[
38
, Lemma 6.1]
).

Lemma (Wt1)
.

Let
K\subset\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\infty})
be a sufficiently small subgroup.

(1)

Then for all
g\in\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\infty})
, the group
\overline{\Gamma}_{K,g}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}gKg^{-1}\cap\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(F)
acts freely on
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(F\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}{\mathbb{R}})/K_{\infty}
, and
Y_{K}
is endowed with the structure of smooth manifold of dimension
2r_{1}+3r_{2}
.

(2)

Let
V=\prod_{v}V_{v}
be a normal open compact subgroup of
K
. Then the map
Y_{V}\to Y_{K}
is a Galois covering space, with Galois group
K/V
. ∎

For any
K
, let
C_{K}
be the complex of singular chains on
Y_{K}
with coefficients in
\mathcal{O}
, so that
C_{K}
computes of the homology
\operatorname{H}_{*}(Y_{K},\mathcal{O})
. Then
C_{K}
is quasi-isomorphic to a bounded complex of free
\mathcal{O}
-modules, as so may be viewed as a perfect complex in
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O})
.

13.2.
Double coset operators

Let
G=\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\infty})

Let
C_{c}(G)
be the abelian group of compactly supported continuous functions
f\colon G\to\mathbb{Z}
. For any compact open subgroups
K_{1},K_{2}\subseteq G
, let

\mathcal{H}\left(K_{2}\backslash G/K_{1}\right)=\{f\in C_{c}(G)|f(k_{2}xk_{1})=f(x)\text{ for all }x\in G,k_{1}\in K_{1},k_{2}\in K_{2}\},

which is an additive subgroup of
C_{c}(G)
. For any
\alpha\in G
and any compact opens
K_{1},K_{2}\subseteq G
, let
[K_{2}\alpha K_{1}]\in\mathcal{H}\left(K_{2}\backslash G/K_{1}\right)
denote the indicator function of the double coset
K_{2}\alpha K_{1}=\{k_{2}\alpha k_{1}|k_{1}\in K_{1},k_{2}\in K_{2}\}\subseteq G
. Then
\mathcal{H}\left(K_{2}\backslash G/K_{1}\right)
is clearly a free abelian group generated by the distinct
[K_{2}\alpha K_{1}]
’s.

Moreover we may define a convolution operation

*:\mathcal{H}\left(K_{3}\backslash G/K_{2}\right)\times\mathcal{H}\left(K_{2}\backslash G/K_{1}\right)\to\mathcal{H}\left(K_{3}\backslash G/K_{1}\right)

by

(g*f)(x)=\sum_{K_{2}y\in K_{2}\backslash G}g(xy^{-1})f(y).

This operation is clearly bilinear and associative, and satisfies

[K_{2}]*f=f=f*[K_{1}]

for any
f\in\mathcal{H}\left(K_{2}\backslash G/K_{1}\right)
. In particular, this makes
\mathcal{H}\left(K\backslash G/K\right)
into a ring.

Moreover it is well known that the convolution operation is given by

[K_{3}\beta K_{2}]*[K_{2}\alpha K_{1}]=\sum_{K_{1}\gamma K_{3}}c_{\gamma}[K_{3}\gamma K_{1}]

where the sum runs over all double cosets
K_{3}\gamma K_{1}\subseteq G
and

c_{\gamma}=\#\{(i,j)|\beta_{j}\alpha_{i}K_{1}=\gamma K_{1}\}

where
\{\alpha_{i}\}
and
\{\beta_{j}\}
are (finite) sets of coset representatives,
K_{2}\alpha K_{1}=\sqcup_{i}\alpha_{i}K_{1}
and
K_{3}\beta K_{2}=\sqcup_{j}\beta_{j}K_{2}
.

Let
K_{1},K_{2}\subseteq\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\infty})
be any compact open subgroups and
\alpha\in\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\infty})
any element. we define a map
[K_{2}\alpha K_{1}]:C_{K_{1}}\to C_{K_{2}}
in
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O})
as follows.

Let
L=\alpha K_{1}\alpha^{-1}\cap K_{2}
, which is also a compact open subgroup of
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\infty})
. Define maps
p_{1}\colon Y_{L}\to Y_{K_{1}}
and
p_{2}\colon Y_{L}\to Y_{K_{2}}
by
p_{1}([x]_{L})=[x\alpha]_{K_{1}}
and
p_{2}([x]_{L})=[x]_{K_{2}}
for
x\in\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\infty})
(where for any
U
and any
y\in\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\infty})
,
[y]_{U}
denotes the equivalence class of
y
in
Y_{U}=\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(F)\backslash\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/UK_{\infty}
). Note that the map
p_{1}
is well-defined as
\alpha^{-1}L\alpha\subseteq K_{1}
.

The maps
p_{i}\colon Y_{L}\to Y_{K_{i}}
for
i=1,2
are both finite-to-one maps of topological spaces (and are covering maps in the case where the
K_{i}
’s are sufficiently small, Lemma
(Wt1)
). Thus they induce maps
p_{i,*}:C_{L}\to C_{K_{i}}
and
p_{i}^{*}\colon C_{K_{i}}\to C_{L}
.

Define the
double coset operator

[K_{2}\alpha K_{1}]\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O})}(C_{K_{1}},C_{K_{2}})
as the composition
C_{K_{1}}\xrightarrow{p_{1}^{*}}C_{L}\xrightarrow{p_{2,*}}C_{K_{2}}
. As shown in
[
45
, Lemma 2.19]
2
2

2

Technically
[
45
]
only proves this in the case when
K_{1}=K_{2}
. However their methods give the result stated here without any significant modifications.
we have

Lemma (Wt1)
.

The assignment
[K_{2}\alpha K_{1}]\mapsto[K_{2}\alpha K_{1}]
defines an additive group homomorphism
\mathcal{H}\left(K_{2}\backslash G/K_{1}\right)\to\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O})}(C_{K_{1}},C_{K_{2}})
such that the map

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O})}(C_{K_{2}},C_{K_{3}})\times\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O})}(C_{K_{1}},C_{K_{2}})\to\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O})}(C_{K_{1}},C_{K_{3}})

induced by convolution
*\colon\mathcal{H}\left(K_{3}\backslash G/K_{2}\right)\times\mathcal{H}\left(K_{2}\backslash G/K_{1}\right)\to\mathcal{H}\left(K_{3}\backslash G/K_{1}\right)
is just function composition.

In particular,
\mathcal{H}\left(K\backslash G/K\right)\to\operatorname{End}_{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O})}(C_{K})
is a ring homomorphism. ∎

13.3.
Hecke algebras

From now on, take a compact open subgroup
K=\prod_{v}K_{v}\subseteq\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\infty})
, and let
S
be a finite set of places such that for each
v\not\in S
,
K_{v}
is a maximal compact subgroup of
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(F_{v})
(
S
need not be the minimal set with these properties). For each
v\not\in S
, let
T_{v}\in\operatorname{End}_{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O})}(C_{K})
be the double coset operator

T_{v}=\left[K\begin{pmatrix}\varpi_{v}&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}K\right]

if
v\not\in S
and
K_{1}(v)\subseteq K_{v}\subseteq K_{0}(v)
and
d\in K_{0}(v)/K_{v}\hookrightarrow(\mathcal{O}_{F}/v)^{\times}
let
U_{v},\langle d\rangle\in\operatorname{End}_{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O})}(C_{K})
denote the double coset operators

\displaystyle U_{v}

\displaystyle=\left[K\begin{pmatrix}\varpi_{v}&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}K\right]

and

\displaystyle\langle d\rangle_{v}

\displaystyle=\left[K\begin{pmatrix}\widetilde{d}&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}K\right]

for any lift
\widetilde{d}\in\mathcal{O}_{F,v}^{\times}
of
d
.

Define
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K)=\mathcal{O}[T_{v}|v\not\in S]\subseteq\operatorname{End}_{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O})}(C_{K})
, which is well known to be a finite, commutative
\mathcal{O}
-algebra. We state a conjecture about existence of Galois representations attached to Hecke eigenclasses (which might be torsion) arising from the cohomology of
C_{K}
. In the case when
F
is a CM field, part (i) is known by the work of Scholze and part (ii) is known for sufficiently large
\ell
; see
[
45
, Theorem 1.1, Theorem 1.3]
.
This is similar to Conjecture A of
[
16
]
.

Conjecture A
.

Let
K=\prod_{v}K_{v}\subseteq\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\infty})
be a compact open subgroup, and let
S
be any finite set of places such that for each
v\not\in S
,
K_{v}
is a maximal compact subgroup of
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(F_{v})
. Then:

(i)

For every maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}
of
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K)
, there is a semisimple Galois representation
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}\colon G_{F}\to\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{T}^{S}(K)/\mathfrak{m})
such that for all
v\not\in S
,
v\nmid\ell
,
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{G_{F_{v}}}
is unramified and
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}(\Frob_{v})
has characteristic polynomial
x^{2}-T_{v}x+\Nm(v)
.

(ii)

Furthermore there is a lift of
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}
to a representation

\rho_{K}\colon G_{F}\to\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{T}^{S}(K)_{\mathfrak{m}})

such that for all
v\not\in S
,
v\nmid\ell
,
\rho_{K}|_{G_{F_{v}}}
is unramified and
\rho_{K}(\Frob_{v})
has characteristic polynomial
x^{2}-T_{v}x+\Nm(v)
.

Remark (Wt1)
.

Note that if we assumed in (ii) above the weaker statement that, for a
\mathbb{T}\in{\mathrm{CNL}}_{\mathcal{O}}
with an inclusion
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K)_{\mathfrak{m}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{T}
, there is a lift of
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}
to a representation
\rho_{K}\colon G_{F}\to\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{T})
such that for all
v\not\in S
,
v
not above
\ell
,
\rho_{K}|_{G_{F_{v}}}
is unramified and
\rho_{K}(\Frob_{v})
has characteristic polynomial
x^{2}-T_{v}x+\Nm(v)
, then the stronger hypothesis in (ii) that the representation can be chosen to take values in
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{T}^{S}(K)_{\mathfrak{m}})
follows provided the residual representation
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}
is irreducible. This follows upon using the Chebotarev density theorem as traces of
\Frob_{v}
for
v\not\in S
,
v\nmid\ell
, with
S
a finite set of places, are in
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K)_{\mathfrak{m}}
.

We say that a maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}\subseteq\mathbb{T}^{S}(K)
is
non-Eisenstein
if the representation
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}
is absolutely irreducible.
In what follows we often use the lemma below, which is a consequence of the Chebotarev density theorem (see
[
38
, Lemma 6.20]
).

Lemma (Wt1)
.

Assume Conjecture
A
. Then for any finite set of places
S^{\prime}
containing
S
, the natural map
\mathbb{T}^{S’}(K)_{\mathfrak{m}}\to\mathbb{T}^{S}(K)_{\mathfrak{m}}
is an isomorphism when
\mathfrak{m}
is non-Eisenstein.

Thus if
\mathfrak{m}
is non-Eisenstein the localization
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K)_{\mathfrak{m}}
does not depend on
S
, and so we often use
\mathbb{T}(K)_{\mathfrak{m}}
to denote this localization, eliding
S
in the notation. As shown in
[
38
]
, for any maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}\subseteq\mathbb{T}(K)
the localization
(C_{K})_{\mathfrak{m}}
is a direct summand of
C_{K}
and we have
\operatorname{H}_{*}(Y_{K},\mathcal{O})_{\mathfrak{m}}=\operatorname{H}_{*}(C_{K})_{\mathfrak{m}}=\operatorname{H}_{*}((C_{K})_{\mathfrak{m}})
. Given a maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}\subseteq\mathbb{T}^{S}(K)
and any compact open
K^{\prime}\subseteq K
, we also use
\mathfrak{m}\subseteq\mathbb{T}^{S}(K^{\prime})
to denote the preimage of
\mathfrak{m}
under the map
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K^{\prime})\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{T}^{S}(K)
.

In what follows we assume that
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}
is such that
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{F(\zeta_{\ell})}
is irreducible. The result below is easy to prove; see
[
35
, Lemma 12.3]
for the proof of the first part.

Lemma (Wt1)
.

Let
\overline{\rho}:G_{F}\to\mathrm{GL}_{2}(k)
be such that
\overline{\rho}|_{F(\zeta_{\ell})}
is irreducible.

(i)

Then there exists a place
t\notin S
such that

\displaystyle\tr\overline{\rho}(\Frob_{t})/\det\overline{\rho}(\Frob_{t})\neq(1+\Nm(t))^{2}/\Nm(t)

\displaystyle\Nm(t)\not\equiv 1\pmod{\ell}\,,

and
\Nm(t)>4^{[F:\mathbb{Q}]}
.

(ii)

For such places
t
the (unrestricted) deformation ring
R_{t}^{\square}=R_{t}^{\rm min}
, and is isomorphic to a power series ring over
\mathcal{O}
of relative dimension 3.

(iii)

For such places
t
, the compact open subgroup

\displaystyle K_{1}(t^{2})

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{F})\middle|c\in t^{2},ad^{-1}=1\pmod{t^{2}}\right\}\subseteq\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{F})

is sufficiently small. ∎

In what follows we apply the lemma for
\overline{\rho}=\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}
and always impose level structures at
t
so that we work with open compact subgroups
K
of
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\infty})
that are sufficiently small. Because no lift of
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}
is ramified at
t
we do not need to explicitly allow the Galois deformations of
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}
we consider below to ramify at
t
. (See 6.5.1, Lemma 6.5.2, and Lemma 6.5.2 of
[
1
]
for similar considerations.)

13.4.
Sufficiently small level subgroups

We fix a prime
t
as in Lemma
(Wt1)
. For any nonzero ideal
\mathcal{N}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{F}
define the following compact open subgroups of
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\infty})
:

\displaystyle K_{0}(\mathcal{N})

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{F})\middle|c\in\mathcal{N}t^{2},ad^{-1}=1\pmod{t^{2}}\right\}\subseteq\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{F})

\displaystyle K_{1}(\mathcal{N})

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{F})\middle|c\in\mathcal{N}t^{2},ad^{-1}=1\pmod{\mathcal{N}t^{2}}\right\}\subseteq\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{F})

so that
K_{1}(\mathcal{N})\unlhd K_{0}(\mathcal{N})
and
K_{0}(\mathcal{N})/K_{1}(\mathcal{N})\cong(\mathcal{O}_{F}/\mathcal{N})^{\times}
via the map

\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\mapsto ad^{-1}\pmod{\mathcal{N}}\,.

Also define
K_{\Delta}(\mathcal{N})
to be the smallest intermediate subgroup

K_{1}(\mathcal{N})\leq K_{\Delta}(\mathcal{N})\leq K_{0}(\mathcal{N})

for which
|K_{0}(\mathcal{N})/K_{\Delta}(\mathcal{N})|
is an
\ell^{th}
power.

In the case when
\mathcal{N}=v^{e}
for some prime
v
and some
e\geq 0
, we also sometimes use
K_{0}(v^{e})
,
K_{1}(v^{e})
and
K_{\Delta}(v^{e})
to denote the compact open subgroups
K_{0}(v^{e})\cap\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{F,v})
,
K_{1}(v^{e})\cap\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{F,v})
and
K_{\Delta}(v^{e})\cap\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{F,v})
of
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(F_{v})
.

For any nonzero ideal
\mathcal{N}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{F}
we write
Y_{0}(\mathcal{N})=Y_{K_{0}(\mathcal{N})}
. Also for an ideal
Q
of
\mathcal{O}_{F}
prime to
\mathcal{N}
, write
Y_{0,\Delta}(\mathcal{N},Q)=Y_{K_{0}(\mathcal{N})\cap K_{\Delta}(Q)}
. Write
C_{0}(\mathcal{N})
and
C_{0,\Delta}(\mathcal{N},Q)
for the corresponding prefect complexes in
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O})
.

(Thus we are imposing level structure at
t
for the compact open subgroups
K
of
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\infty})
we consider; we suppress this from the notation to make it less clumsy and hope this will not be misleading for the reader.)

Duality

From now on fix a nonzero-ideal
\mathcal{N}_{\varnothing}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{F}
and a non-Eisenstein maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}\subseteq\mathbb{T}^{S}(K_{0}(\mathcal{N}_{\varnothing}))
.
Assume that
N(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}})=\mathcal{N}_{\varnothing}
(i.e.
\mathcal{N}_{\varnothing}
is the
minimal level
for
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}
). By enlarging
\mathcal{O}
if necessary we may assume that
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K)/\mathfrak{m}\cong k
, and so
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}
is a representation
G_{F}\to\mathrm{GL}_{2}(k)
.

Proposition (Wt1)
.

Assume Conjecture
A
holds. Then for any compact open
K=\prod_{v}K_{v}\subseteq K_{0}(\mathcal{N}_{\varnothing})
, there is a natural derived
\mathbb{T}(K)_{\mathfrak{m}}
-isomorphism

(C_{K})_{\mathfrak{m}}\cong\operatorname{RHom}_{\mathcal{O}}((C_{K})_{\mathfrak{m}},\mathcal{O})[2r_{1}+3r_{2}]=(C_{K})_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}

in
{\mathsf{D}}_{\mathbb{T}(K)_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\mathcal{O})
. Moreover, for any
K_{1},K_{2}\subseteq K_{0}(\mathcal{N}_{\varnothing})
the adjoint of the double coset operator
[K_{2}\alpha K_{1}]\colon(C_{K_{1}})_{\mathfrak{m}}\to(C_{K_{2}})_{\mathfrak{m}}
with respect to these isomorphisms is given by the double coset operator
[K_{1}\alpha^{-1}K_{2}]\colon(C_{K_{2}})_{\mathfrak{m}}\to(C_{K_{1}})_{\mathfrak{m}}
.

Proof.

By
[
45
, Proposition 3.7]
and
[
45
, Theorem 4.2]
there is a Verdier duality isomorphism
(C_{K})_{\mathfrak{m}}\cong\operatorname{RHom}_{\mathcal{O}}((C_{K})_{\mathfrak{m}},\mathcal{O}[2r_{1}+3r_{2}])
sending each double coset operator
[K_{2}\alpha K_{1}]
to
[K_{1}\alpha^{-1}K_{2}]
. Here we are using the fact that condition
\spadesuit
of
loc. cit.
holds in the case
m=2
by Conjecture
A
and in the case
m=1
by class field theory.

In particular this is
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K)
-equivariant as it is easy to check that

\displaystyle K\begin{pmatrix}\varpi_{v}^{-1}&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}K

\displaystyle=K\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\varpi_{v}^{-1}&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}K

\displaystyle=K\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&\varpi_{v}^{-1}\end{pmatrix}K

\displaystyle=K\begin{pmatrix}\varpi_{v}&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}K,

whenever
K_{v}=\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{F,v})
, and so each
T_{v}
for
v\not\in S
is self-adjoint.
∎

The isomorphism from Proposition
(Wt1)
is not in general equivariant for the Hecke operators
U_{v}
and
\langle d\rangle_{v}
. However, it is easy to modify this isomorphism to make it equivariant for all Hecke operators.

From now on we restrict our attention to compact open subgroups
K
in the form
K=\prod_{v}K_{v}\subseteq K_{0}(\mathcal{N}_{\varnothing})
where for all
v
either
K_{v}=\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{F,v})
or
K_{1}(v)\leq K_{v}\leq K_{0}(v)
. For each
v
, let

w_{v}=\left[K\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
\varpi_{v}&0\end{pmatrix}K\right]

if
K_{1}(v)\leq K_{v}\leq K_{0}(v)
and let
w_{v}=1
if
K_{v}=\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{F,v})
.
Observe that
K_{v}\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
\varpi_{v}&0\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
\varpi_{v}&0\end{pmatrix}K_{v}
for
K_{1}(v)\leq K_{v}\leq K_{0}(v)
and
K_{v}=\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{F,v})
. It is easy to verify from this that

[K_{3}\beta K_{2}]*\left[K_{2}\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
\varpi_{v}&0\end{pmatrix}K_{1}\right]=\left[K_{3}\beta\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
\varpi_{v}&0\end{pmatrix}K_{1}\right]

and

\left[K_{3}\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
\varpi_{v}&0\end{pmatrix}K_{2}\right]*\left[K_{2}\alpha K_{1}\right]=\left[K_{3}\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
\varpi_{v}&0\end{pmatrix}\alpha K_{1}\right]

It follows that
w_{v}^{2}=1
for all
v
and
w_{v}w_{v^{\prime}}=w_{v^{\prime}}w_{v}
for any
v
and
v^{\prime}
. Moreover by definition,
w_{v}=1
whenever
K_{v}=\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{F,v})
(and hence in particular,
w_{v}=1
for
v\not\in S
). Hence the operator
w_{K}=\prod_{v\in S}w_{v}\in\operatorname{End}_{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O})}((C_{K})_{\mathfrak{m}})
is well-defined and independent of
S
. As
w_{K}^{2}=1
,
w_{K}
is an automorphism in
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O})
. So now we may define an isomorphism
\varphi_{K}\colon(C_{K})_{\mathfrak{m}}\xrightarrow{\sim}(C_{K})_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}
by

\varphi_{K}:(C_{K})_{\mathfrak{m}}\xrightarrow{w_{K}}(C_{K})_{\mathfrak{m}}\xrightarrow{\sim}(C_{K})_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}

where the second map is the isomorphism from Proposition
(Wt1)
.

Now we have the following:

Proposition (Wt1)
.

The isomorphism
\varphi_{K}\colon(C_{K})_{\mathfrak{m}}\cong(C_{K})_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}
commutes with the actions of all the Hecke operators
T_{v}
,
U_{v}
and
\langle d\rangle_{v}
.

Moreover the adjoint of
[K_{2}\alpha K_{1}]\colon(C_{K_{1}})_{\mathfrak{m}}\to(C_{K_{2}})_{\mathfrak{m}}
with respect to
\varphi_{K_{1}}
and
\varphi_{K_{2}}
is
w_{K_{1}}[K_{1}\alpha^{-1}K_{2}]w_{K_{2}}\colon(C_{K_{2}})_{\mathfrak{m}}\to(C_{K_{1}})_{\mathfrak{m}}
.

Proof.

The last claim is immediate from Proposition
(Wt1)
and the definition of
\varphi_{K}
.

For the first claim, we have
w_{K}T_{v}w_{K}=T_{v}
for
v\not\in S
by the definition of
w_{K}
. The fact that
U_{v}
and
\langle d\rangle_{v}
are self adjoint now follows from the observation that

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
\varpi_{v}&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}a&0\\
0&d\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
\varpi_{v}&0\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
\varpi_{v}&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}a^{-1}&0\\
0&d^{-1}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
\varpi_{v}&0\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}-\varpi_{v}d^{-1}&0\\
0&-\varpi_{v}a^{-1}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}a&0\\
0&d\end{pmatrix}

in
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(F_{v})
, for any
a,d\in F_{v}^{\times}
.
∎

13.5.
Level lowering maps

For this subsection fix a compact open
K
of
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{A})
as before and a place
v
for which
K_{v}=\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{F,v})
. Write
L=K\cap K_{0}(v)
. Define two maps
\pi_{1},\pi_{2}\colon(C_{L})_{\mathfrak{m}}\to(C_{K})_{\mathfrak{m}}
by

\pi_{1}=\left[K\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}L\right]\quad\text{and}\quad\pi_{2}=\left[K\begin{pmatrix}\varpi_{v}&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}L\right]

and write
\pi_{K,v}=\pi_{1}\oplus\pi_{2}:(C_{L})_{\mathfrak{m}}\to(C_{K})_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\oplus 2}
. Let
\pi_{K,v}^{\dagger}\colon(C_{K})_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\oplus 2}\to(C_{L})_{\mathfrak{m}}
be the adjoint of
\pi_{K,v}
with respect to
\varphi_{K}
and
\varphi_{L}
. It is easy to see that

\pi_{K,v}\circ U_{v}=\begin{pmatrix}T_{v}&-1\\
\Nm(v)&0\end{pmatrix}\circ\pi_{K,v}

as morphisms
(C_{L})_{\mathfrak{m}}\to(C_{K})_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\oplus 2}
.

13.6.
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}
and
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q}

Pick disjoint finite sets
T
and
Q
of places of
F
satisfying the conditions from Section
12.2
(which in particular imply that every element of
T
and
Q
is prime to
\mathcal{N}_{\varnothing}
).

For any subset
\Sigma\subseteq T
let
\mathcal{N}_{\Sigma}=\mathcal{N}_{\varnothing}\prod_{v\in\Sigma}v
, where
\mathcal{N}_{\varnothing}
is as above. From now on assume that
T\cup Q\subseteq S
, and
S
contains all primes dividing
\mathcal{N}_{\varnothing}
and the place
t
.
For any
\Sigma\subseteq T
, define
K_{\Sigma}=K_{0}(\mathcal{N}_{\Sigma})
and
K_{\Sigma,Q}=K_{0}(\mathcal{N}_{\Sigma})\cap K_{\Delta}(Q)
. Note that
K_{\Sigma,\varnothing}=K_{\Sigma}
.

Consider the Hecke algebras
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K_{\Sigma})
and
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K_{\Sigma,Q})
. Let
\mathfrak{m}\subseteq\mathbb{T}^{S}(K_{0}(\mathcal{N}_{\varnothing}))
be a non-Eisenstein maximal ideal. Assume further that
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{G_{F(\zeta_{\ell})}}
is absolutely irreducible.

Again, for any compact open
K\subseteq K_{0}(\mathcal{N}_{\Sigma})
, let
\mathfrak{m}\subseteq\mathbb{T}^{S}(K)
denote the preimage of
\mathfrak{m}
. Write
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}=\mathbb{T}^{S}(K_{\Sigma})_{\mathfrak{m}}
and
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q}=\mathbb{T}^{S}(K_{\Sigma,Q})_{\mathfrak{m}}
.

Now define the full Hecke algebras:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{S}(K_{\Sigma})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{T}^{S}(K_{\Sigma})[\{U_{v}\}_{v\in\Sigma}]\subseteq\operatorname{End}_{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O})}(C_{K_{\Sigma}})

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{S}(K_{\Sigma,Q})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{T}^{S}(K_{\Sigma})[\{U_{v}\}_{v\in\Sigma\cup Q},\{\langle d\rangle_{v}\}_{v\in Q,d\in\Delta_{v}}]\subseteq\operatorname{End}_{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O})}(C_{K_{\Sigma,Q}}).

These are commutative
\mathcal{O}
-algebras, finite over
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K_{\Sigma})
and
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K_{\Sigma,Q})
. Again note that
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{S}(K_{\Sigma,\varnothing})=\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{S}(K_{\Sigma})
.
We define ideals
\mathfrak{m}_{\Sigma,Q}\subseteq\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{S}(K_{\Sigma,Q})
lying over
\mathfrak{m}\subseteq\mathbb{T}^{S}(K_{\Sigma,Q})
by specifying that

U_{v}-\epsilon_{v}
(for
v\in\Sigma
) and
U_{v}-\gamma_{1,v}
(for
v\in Q
) are in
\mathfrak{m}_{\Sigma,Q}
. Then it is easy to see that these are indeed maximal ideals and that the ideal
\mathfrak{m}_{\Sigma,Q}\subseteq\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{S}(K_{\Sigma,Q})
lies over the ideal
\mathfrak{m}_{\Sigma,\varnothing}\subseteq\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{S}(K_{\Sigma})
and for
\Sigma_{1}\subseteq\Sigma_{2}
, the ideal
\mathfrak{m}_{\Sigma_{2},Q}\subseteq\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{S}(K_{\Sigma_{2},Q})
lies over the ideal
\mathfrak{m}_{\Sigma_{1},Q}\subseteq\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{S}(K_{\Sigma_{1},Q})
.

The following conjecture asserts that the Galois representations of Conjecture
A
satisfy local-global compatibility. Many cases of this conjecture (up to the issue of going modulo a nilpotent ideal) are known from
[
1
, §3, §4]
).

Conjecture B
.

For any
\Sigma
and
Q
, the representations
\rho_{\Sigma,Q}\colon G_{F}\to\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q})
stemming from Conjecture
A
arise from the corresponding universal representation
G_{F}\to\mathrm{GL}_{2}(R_{\Sigma,Q})
via maps
R_{\Sigma,Q}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q}
and satisfy the following properties:

(1)

\tr\rho_{\Sigma,Q}(\Frob_{v})=T_{v}
for all
v\not\in S
.

(2)

If
R_{\Sigma,Q}
is given the
\mathcal{O}[\Delta_{Q}]
structure arising from Proposition
(Wt1)
, then the composition of maps
R_{\Sigma,Q}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q}\to\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{S}(K_{\Sigma,Q})_{\mathfrak{m}_{\Sigma,Q}}
is an
\mathcal{O}[\Delta_{Q}]
-algebra homomorphism. Furthermore
\chi_{1,v}(\varphi_{v})
maps to
U_{v}
for
v\in Q
for a suitable lift
\varphi_{v}
of
\Frob_{v}
.

(3)

For
v\in\Sigma
, writing
B_{v}\in\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}^{\square}
for the image of
B_{v}\in R_{v}^{{\rm uni}(\epsilon_{v})}
under the map

R_{v}^{{\rm uni}(\epsilon_{v})}\hookrightarrow R_{\Sigma,\loc}\to R_{\Sigma}^{\square}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}^{\square},

we have
(B_{v})=(\epsilon_{v}U_{v}-1)
as ideals of
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}^{\square}
.

(4)

The diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{\Sigma,Q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{\Sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{\Sigma_{2},Q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma_{2},Q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{\Sigma_{1},Q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma_{1},Q}}

(for
\Sigma_{1}\subseteq\Sigma_{2}\subseteq T
) commute.

Note that the assumption that
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q}
is a quotient of
R_{\Sigma,Q}
implies that
\rho_{\Sigma,Q}
satisfies all of the necessary local-global compatibility assumptions (such as the fact that it is flat at all primes
v|\ell
).

Lemma (Wt1)
.

We have that
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{S}(K_{\Sigma,Q})_{\mathfrak{m}_{\Sigma,Q}}=\mathbb{T}^{S}(K_{\Sigma,Q})_{\mathfrak{m}}=\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q}
.

Proof.

This follows from the above Conjecture
B
as from it we see that
U_{v},v\in\Sigma\cup Q
and
\{\langle d\rangle_{v}\}_{v\in Q,d\in\Delta_{v}}\subseteq\operatorname{End}_{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O})}(C_{K_{\Sigma,Q}})
belong to
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q}
.
∎

We will write
\mathfrak{m}_{\Sigma}=\mathfrak{m}_{\Sigma,\varnothing}
. Lemma
(Wt1)
implies that we may now treat
U_{v}
for
v\in\Sigma\cup Q
and
\langle d\rangle_{v}
for
v\in Q
and
d\in\Delta_{v}
as elements of
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q}
. In particular there is now a natural
\mathcal{O}
-algebra homomorphism
\mathcal{O}[\Delta_{Q}]=\otimes_{v\in Q}\mathcal{O}[\Delta_{v}]\to\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q}
, given by
d\mapsto\langle d\rangle_{v}
for
d\in\Delta_{v}
, and so we may view
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q}
as a
\mathcal{O}[\Delta_{Q}]
-algebra.

Define the complexes
C_{\Sigma}=(C_{K_{\Sigma}})_{\mathfrak{m}_{\Sigma}}
and
C_{\Sigma,Q}=(C_{K_{\Sigma,Q}})_{\mathfrak{m}_{\Sigma,Q}}
in
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O})
. Note that these are not the same as the complexes
(C_{K_{\Sigma}})_{\mathfrak{m}}
and
(C_{K_{\Sigma,Q}})_{\mathfrak{m}}
, as we are localizing with respect to the action of a larger ring.
In particular we naturally have
C_{\Sigma}\in{\mathsf{D}}_{\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}}(\mathcal{O})
and
C_{\Sigma,Q}\in{\mathsf{D}}_{\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q}}(\mathcal{O})
.

Proposition (Wt1)
.

For each
\Sigma\subseteq T
and each
Q
we have:

•

The action
\mathcal{O}[\Delta_{Q}]\to\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q}\to\operatorname{End}_{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O})}(C_{\Sigma,Q})
gives
C_{\Sigma,Q}
the structure of a perfect complex of
\mathcal{O}[\Delta_{Q}]
-modules.

•

\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}[\Delta_{Q}]}\mathcal{O}\cong\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}

•

C_{\Sigma,Q}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{\mathcal{O}[\Delta_{Q}]}\mathcal{O}\cong C_{\Sigma}
in
{\mathsf{D}}_{\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}}(\mathcal{O})
.

Proof.

This is proved in
[
38
, §5]
in a more general context. It is also proved (for
\mathrm{GL}_{2}
) in
[
16
, Lemma 9.5]
.
∎

Now fix
v\in T\smallsetminus\Sigma
and consider the map

\pi_{K,v}=\pi_{1}\oplus\pi_{2}\colon(C_{K_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},Q}})_{\mathfrak{m}_{\Sigma,Q}}\longrightarrow(C_{K_{\Sigma,Q}})_{\mathfrak{m}_{\Sigma,Q}}^{\oplus 2}

arising from Section
13.5
(for
K=K_{\Sigma,Q}
, after localizing at
\mathfrak{m}_{\Sigma,Q}
). We claim that this induces a map
\pi_{v}\colon C_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},Q}\to C_{\Sigma,Q}
. To see this we consider the operator

\begin{pmatrix}T_{v}&-1\\
\Nm(v)&0\end{pmatrix}

that we call
U_{v}^{\prime}
, acting on
(C_{K_{\Sigma,Q}})_{\mathfrak{m}_{\Sigma,Q}}^{\oplus 2}
. Consider the maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}_{\Sigma,Q}
of

\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q}[U_{v}^{\prime}]/(U_{v}^{\prime 2}-T_{v}U^{\prime}_{v}+\Nm(v))

acting on
(C_{K_{\Sigma,Q}})_{\mathfrak{m}_{\Sigma,Q}}^{\oplus 2}
, that extends the maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}_{\Sigma,Q}
and is given by
(\mathfrak{m}_{\Sigma,Q},U^{\prime}_{v}-\epsilon_{v})
. We consider the localization of
(C_{K_{\Sigma,Q}})_{\mathfrak{m}_{\Sigma,Q}}^{\oplus 2}
at
\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}_{\Sigma,Q}
. This is isomorphic as a
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q}
module to
C_{\Sigma,Q}
. Thus we get a Hecke equivariant (for the
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},Q}
-action) map
\pi_{v}\colon C_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},Q}\to C_{\Sigma,Q}
. It also induces an adjoint map
\pi_{v}^{\dagger}\colon C_{\Sigma,Q}\to C_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},Q}
. (See
[
56
, §2.2]
for similar arguments.)

Note that we may treat
U_{v}
as an element of
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q}
via Lemma
(Wt1)
and the quotient map
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},Q}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q}
. From the relation

\pi_{K,v}\circ U_{v}=\begin{pmatrix}T_{v}&-1\\
\Nm(v)&0\end{pmatrix}\circ\pi_{K,v}

noted in §
13.5

we get that the action of
U^{\prime}_{v}
on
C_{\Sigma,Q}
agrees with the action of
U_{v}\in\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q}
on
C_{\Sigma,Q}
.

Proposition
(Wt1)
and a standard computation gives the following:

Proposition (Wt1)
.

There is a family of isomorphisms

\varphi_{\Sigma,Q}\colon C_{\Sigma,Q}\xrightarrow{\sim}C_{\Sigma,Q}^{\dagger}

in
{\mathsf{D}}_{\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q}}(\mathcal{O})
compatible with the isomorphisms
C_{\Sigma,Q}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{\mathcal{O}[\Delta_{Q}]}\mathcal{O}\cong C_{\Sigma}
.

Moreover for each
v\in T\smallsetminus\Sigma
, if

\pi_{v}^{\dagger}\colon C_{\Sigma,Q}\cong C_{\Sigma,Q}^{\dagger}\to C_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},Q}^{\dagger}\cong C_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},Q}

is the adjoint of
\pi_{v}\colon C_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},Q}\to C_{\Sigma,Q}
, upon using the isomorphisms
\varphi_{\Sigma,Q}
and
\varphi_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},Q}
, then

\displaystyle\pi_{v}\circ\pi_{v}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\simeq U_{v}^{2}-1\in\operatorname{End}_{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O})}(C_{\Sigma,Q})

and

\displaystyle\pi_{v}^{\dagger}\circ\pi_{v}

\displaystyle\simeq U_{v}^{2}-1\in\operatorname{End}_{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O})}(C_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},Q}),

where
\simeq
means up to units in the corresponding Hecke algebras
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q}
and
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},Q}
respectively.

Proof.

The first assertion follows from localizing the results of Proposition
(Wt1)
. To see the second assertion above we can use that (see the proof of
[
46
, Proposition 3.3]
for a similar computation)

\pi_{K,v}\circ(U_{v}^{2}-1)=\pi\circ\pi^{\dagger}\circ\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
-1&T_{v}\end{pmatrix}\circ\pi_{K,v}

as maps
(C_{K_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},Q}})_{\mathfrak{m}}\to(C_{K_{\Sigma,Q}})_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\oplus 2}
.
∎

Conjecture C
.

If
K\subseteq\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\infty})
is any compact open subgroup and
\mathfrak{m}
is a non-Eisenstein maximal ideal, then
\operatorname{H}_{i}(X_{K},k)_{\mathfrak{m}}=0
for
i\not\in[r_{1}+r_{2},r_{1}+2r_{2}]
.

This conjecture is known when
F
is an imaginary quadratic field as then
X_{K}
is a 3-dimensional manifold and
\operatorname{H}_{i}(X_{K},k)_{\mathfrak{m}}=0
for
i=0,3
(as
\mathfrak{m}
is a non-Eisenstein maximal ideal).

13.7.
Level raising and Ihara’s Lemma

Conjecture D
.

Let
K
and
v
be as in Section
13.5
, and assume that
\mathfrak{m}\subseteq\mathbb{T}^{S}(K)
is a
non-Eisenstein
maximal ideal. Let
\pi_{K,v}\colon(C_{K\cap K_{0}(v)})_{\mathfrak{m}}\to(C_{K})_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\oplus 2}
be the level lowering map.

Then the induced map

\operatorname{H}_{r_{1}+r_{2}}(\pi_{K,v})\colon\operatorname{H}_{r_{1}+r_{2}}(Y_{K\cap K_{0}(v)},\mathcal{O})_{\mathfrak{m}}\to\operatorname{H}_{r_{1}+r_{2}}(Y_{K},\mathcal{O})_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\oplus 2}

is surjective.

Note that by Conjecture
C
,
\operatorname{H}_{i}(Y_{K},\mathcal{O})_{\mathfrak{m}}=0
for
i<r_{1}+r_{2}
, and so Conjecture
D
concerns the smallest degree in which the homology
\operatorname{H}_{*}(Y_{K},\mathcal{O})_{\mathfrak{m}}
does not vanish.

As the map
\pi_{v}\colon C_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},Q}\to C_{\Sigma,Q}
constructed above is a localization of the map
\pi_{K_{\Sigma,Q},v}
, we get:

Lemma (Wt1)
.

If Conjecture
D
holds for
K=K_{\Sigma,Q}
then the map

\operatorname{H}_{r_{1}+r_{2}}(\pi_{v})\colon\operatorname{H}_{r_{1}+r_{2}}(C_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},Q})\to\operatorname{H}_{r_{1}+r_{2}}(C_{\Sigma,Q})

is surjective.

Theorem (Wt1)
.

When
F
is an imaginary quadratic field and
\ell>3
, Conjecture
D
is true. Namely the map
\operatorname{H}_{1}(\pi_{K,v})\colon\operatorname{H}_{1}(Y_{K\cap K_{0}(v)},\mathcal{O})_{\mathfrak{m}}\to\operatorname{H}_{1}(Y_{K},\mathcal{O})_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\oplus 2}
is surjective.

Proof.

This is well known; see
[
17
, §4.1]
or
[
40
]
. It is a consequence of the congruence subgroup property of
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{F}[\frac{1}{v}])
for
F
an imaginary quadratic field.
∎

14.
Hecke algebras for weight one modular forms

In this section we work exclusively in Case
(Wt1)
, so restrict to the case
F=\mathbb{Q}
and fix a finite set
\mathfrak{D}
of primes with even cardinality. We consider weight one modular forms defined on a Shimura curve
X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K}
(defined below) of discriminant
\mathfrak{D}
. Frequently, we use
\mathfrak{D}
also to denote the product of all the primes in the set
\mathfrak{D}
. The context will make clear which meaning is intended.

Theorem
H
from the introduction was proved by Calegari
[
18
]
when
\mathfrak{D}=\varnothing
, i.e. the modular curve case, so (in the proofs below) we assume
\mathfrak{D}\neq\varnothing
for convenience. We also fix a prime
\ell>3
which is not in
\mathfrak{D}
and for all
v\in\mathfrak{D}
,
v\not\equiv 1\pmod{\ell}
. Let
E
,
\mathcal{O}
,
\varpi
and
k
be as before.

14.1.
Weight one sheaves and complexes on Shimura curves

Let
D_{\mathfrak{D}}
be the quaternion algebra over
\mathbb{Q}
, ramified precisely at the primes in
\mathfrak{D}
. As
\mathfrak{D}
has even cardinality,
D_{\mathfrak{D}}
is indefinite.

Let
K=\prod_{q}K_{q}\subseteq D_{\mathfrak{D}}^{\times}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\infty})
be a compact open subgroup such that:

•

K_{q}
is the maximal compact subgroup of
D_{\mathfrak{D}}^{\times}(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
when
q\in\mathfrak{D}
;

•

K_{\ell}
is a maximal compact subgroup of
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
;

•

K
is
sufficiently small
, in the sense that
gKg^{-1}\cap D_{\mathfrak{D}}^{\times}(\mathbb{Q})
is torsion free for all
g\in D_{\mathfrak{D}}^{\times}(\AA_{F}^{\infty})
;

•

and the determinant map
K
to
\Pi_{q}\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{*}
is surjective.

A typical such
K
that we use is the subgroup
K_{H,\Delta}^{\mathfrak{D}}(N_{\Sigma},Q)
defined below.

Consider the (compact) Riemann surface

X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}D^{\times}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\left(D^{\times}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\infty})\times\mathcal{H}^{\pm}\right)/K

where
\mathcal{H}^{\pm}:=\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathbb{R}
is the complex upper and lower half planes. Give
X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K}
its canonical structure as an algebraic curve over
\mathbb{Q}
. Let
\mathcal{X}^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K}
be a minimal integral model for
X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K}
over
\mathcal{O}
, and for any
\mathcal{O}
-algebra
A
, let
\mathcal{X}^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K,A}
be the base change of
\mathcal{X}^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K}
to
A
.

For
A=\mathcal{O},E,\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{n}\mathcal{O}
,
let
\omega_{A}
be the sheaf on
X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K}
arising from (applying an idempotent
e
as in
[
24
, §4]
which satisfies the condition
e^{*}=e
for a Rossati involution
*
to)
\pi_{*}\Omega_{\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{X}^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K,A}}
for the universal abelian surface
\pi\colon\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{X}^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K,A}
. For any
n\geq 1
define the
coherent homology group
as in
[
16
, §7.2]
by

\operatorname{H}_{i}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K},\omega_{\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{n}\mathcal{O}}):=\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{H}^{i}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K},\omega_{\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{n}\mathcal{O}}),E/\mathcal{O})

and define

\operatorname{H}_{i}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K},\omega_{\mathcal{O}}):=\varprojlim_{n}\operatorname{H}_{i}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K},\omega_{\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{n}\mathcal{O}}).

Note that if we define the sheaf
\omega_{E/\mathcal{O}}
to be the direct limit over
n
of the sheaves
\omega_{\varpi^{-n}\mathcal{O}/\mathcal{O}}
then

\operatorname{H}_{i}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K},\omega_{\mathcal{O}})=\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{H}^{i}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K},\omega_{E/\mathcal{O}}),E/\mathcal{O})

By the work of
[
16
, §7.2]
, one can construct perfect complexes
C_{K}^{\mathfrak{D}}
in
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O})
computing
\operatorname{H}_{i}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K},\omega_{\mathcal{O}})
, which will play a similar in our argument to the complexes
C_{K}
from Section
13
.

Proposition (Wt1)
.

Let
K\subseteq D^{\times}_{\mathfrak{D}}(\mathbb{A}^{\infty}_{\mathbb{Q}})
be a compact open subgroup satisfying the above properties. Then

(1)

Then there is a perfect complex
C_{K}^{\mathfrak{D}}
in
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O})
such that for all
i
:

(a)

\operatorname{H}_{i}(C_{K}^{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{\mathcal{O}}k)=0
for
i\not\in[0,1]
;

(b)

\operatorname{H}_{i}(C_{K}^{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{n})\cong\operatorname{H}_{i}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K},\omega_{\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{n}\mathcal{O}})
for all
n\geq 0
;

(c)

\operatorname{H}_{i}(C_{K}^{\mathfrak{D}})\cong\operatorname{H}_{i}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K},\omega_{\mathcal{O}})
.

(2)

Let
K_{\Delta}\subseteq D^{\times}_{\mathfrak{D}}(\mathbb{A}^{\infty}_{\mathbb{Q}})
be another compact open subgroup satisfying the above properties we with
K_{\Delta}\unlhd K
and
\Delta:=K/K_{\Delta}
a finite abelian group of
\ell
-power order. Then there is a perfect complex
C^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K_{\Delta}}
in
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O}[\Delta])
such that for all
i
:

(a)

\operatorname{H}_{i}(C_{K_{\Delta}}^{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{\mathcal{O}[\Delta]}k)=0
for
i\not\in[0,1]
;

(b)

\operatorname{H}_{i}(C_{K_{\Delta}}^{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{\mathcal{O}[\Delta]}\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{n}[\Delta])\cong\operatorname{H}_{i}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K_{\Delta}},\omega_{\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{n}\mathcal{O}})
as
\mathcal{O}[\Delta]
-modules for all
n\geq 0
;

(c)

\operatorname{H}_{i}(C_{K_{\Delta}}^{\mathfrak{D}})\cong\operatorname{H}_{i}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K_{\Delta}},\omega_{\mathcal{O}})
as
\mathcal{O}[\Delta]
-modules;

(d)

There is a quasi-isomorphism
C_{K_{\Delta}}^{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{\mathcal{O}[\Delta]}\mathcal{O}\cong C_{K}^{\mathfrak{D}}
in
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O})
,

where the
\mathcal{O}[\Delta]
-module structures on
\operatorname{H}_{i}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K_{\Delta}},\omega_{\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{n}\mathcal{O}})
and
\operatorname{H}_{i}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K_{\Delta}},\omega_{\mathcal{O}})
are induced by the action of
\Delta
on
X_{K_{\Delta}}^{\mathfrak{D}}
.

Proof.

Note that (1) is simply the special case of (2) with
K_{\Delta}=K
, so it is enough to prove (2). The assumption that
K
is sufficiently small implies that the quotient map
X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K_{\Delta}}\to X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K}
is étale with Galois group
\Delta
. Thus the work of
[
16
, §7.2]
constructs (using Čech complexes associated to an affine open cover of
X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K}
) for each
n\geq 1
a perfect complex
C^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K_{\Delta},n}
of
(\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{n}\mathcal{O})[\Delta]
-modules with:

\displaystyle\operatorname{H}_{i}(C^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K_{\Delta},n})

\displaystyle\cong\operatorname{H}_{i}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K_{\Delta}},\omega_{\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{n}\mathcal{O}}),

\displaystyle C^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K_{\Delta},n+1}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{(\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{n+1}\mathcal{O})[\Delta]}(\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{n}\mathcal{O})[\Delta]

\displaystyle\cong C^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K_{\Delta},n},

\displaystyle C^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K_{\Delta},n}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{(\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{n}\mathcal{O})[\Delta]}(\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{n}\mathcal{O})

\displaystyle\cong C^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K,n}.

Now
[
38
, Lemmas 2.13, 2.14]
produce a perfect complex
C^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K_{\Delta}}
in
\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O}[\Delta])
with
C^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K_{\Delta}}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{\mathcal{O}[\Delta]}(\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{n}\mathcal{O})[\Delta]\cong C^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K_{\Delta},n}
and

\operatorname{H}_{i}(C^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K_{\Delta}})\cong\varprojlim_{n}\operatorname{H}_{i}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K},\omega_{\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{n}\mathcal{O}})=\operatorname{H}_{i}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K},\omega_{\mathcal{O}}).

It is now straightforward to verify that
C^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K_{\Delta}}
satisfies all of the listed properties. Note that property (a) follows as
\operatorname{H}_{i}(C_{K_{\Delta}}^{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{\mathcal{O}[\Delta]}k)=\operatorname{H}_{i}(C_{K_{\Delta},1}^{\mathfrak{D}})=\operatorname{H}_{i}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K_{\Delta}},\omega_{k})
vanishes for
i\neq 0,1
.
∎

One can now define actions of double coset operators on the complexes
C^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K}
by the procedure described in
[
16
, §7.2]
(combined with the work of
[
38
, §2.4]
to lift the actions of the double coset operators on
C^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K,n}
to an action on
C^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K}
).

We will now use this to define Hecke algebras analogously to Section
13.3
. Let
S
be a set of primes including the primes in
\mathfrak{D}
, the primes
v
for which
K_{v}
is not maximal compact, and the prime
\ell
. For
v\not\in S
, let
T_{v}\in\operatorname{End}_{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O})}(C_{K}^{\mathfrak{D}})
denote the double coset operator

T_{v}=\left[K\begin{pmatrix}\varpi_{v}&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}K\right]

also for
v\in S\setminus\mathfrak{D}
with
v\neq\ell
,
K_{1}(v)\leq K_{v}\leq K_{0}(v)
and
d\in K_{0}(v)/K_{v}\hookrightarrow(\mathcal{O}_{F}/v)^{\times}
, let
U_{v},\langle d\rangle_{v}\in\operatorname{End}_{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O})}(C_{K}^{\mathfrak{D}})
denote the double coset operators

\displaystyle U_{v}

\displaystyle=\left[K\begin{pmatrix}\varpi_{v}&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}K\right]

and

\displaystyle\langle d\rangle_{v}

\displaystyle=\left[K\begin{pmatrix}\widetilde{d}&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}K\right]

for any lift
\widetilde{d}\in\mathbb{Z}_{v}^{\times}
of
d
. Set

\mathbb{T}^{S}(K)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{O}[T_{v}|v\not\in S]\subseteq\operatorname{End}_{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O})}(C^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K})

which is a finite, commutative
\mathcal{O}
-algebra.

Before going on, we should address a subtlety with the definition of
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K)
. We have defined
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K)
as a subring of
\operatorname{End}_{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O})}(C^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K})
, whereas most sources (including
[
16
]
, which we rely on heavily in the proof of Theorem
(Wt1)
) define it as a subring of
\operatorname{End}_{\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{H}^{0}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K},\omega_{E/\mathcal{O}}))=\operatorname{End}_{\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{H}_{0}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K},\omega_{\mathcal{O}}))=\operatorname{End}_{\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{H}^{0}(C^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K}))
.
A priori, this means that the classical Hecke algebra will only be a quotient of
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K)
, which makes it difficult to apply available results in our setting.

Fortunately the result below shows that our definition of
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K)
agrees with the classical definition, and so we may freely use results proved using the classical one.

Proposition (Wt1)
.

The ring
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K)\subseteq\operatorname{End}_{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O})}(C^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K})
acts faithfully on
\operatorname{H}^{0}(C^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K})
.

For the remainder of this section we let define
(-)^{\dagger}\colon{\mathsf{D}}_{A}(\mathcal{O})\to{\mathsf{D}}_{A}(\mathcal{O})
by

C^{\dagger}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{RHom}_{\mathcal{O}}(C,\mathcal{O})[1].

(more specifically, will take
d=0
and
l_{0}=1
in the results of Sections
10
and
11
). Proposition
(Wt1)
is a consequence of the following analogue of Proposition
(Wt1)
:

Proposition (Wt1)
.

For each
K
, there is a natural derived
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K)
-equivariant isomorphism,

C^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K}\cong\operatorname{RHom}_{\mathcal{O}}(C^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K},\mathcal{O})[1]=(C^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K})^{\dagger}

in
{\mathsf{D}}_{\mathbb{T}^{S}(K)}(\mathcal{O})
.

Proof.

The existence of this isomorphism follows from the arguments of
[
10
, Section 3.2]
, using Serre duality applied to the sheaves
\omega_{A}
and the Kodaira-Spencer isomorphism, which gives
\omega_{A}^{\otimes 2}=\Omega_{A}
where
\Omega_{A}
is the canonical sheaf on the Shimura curve
X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma,Q}
. After this the Proposition
(Wt1)
follows from arguments analogous to the ones given in Section
13
(see proof of Proposition
(Wt1)
).
∎

In particular, this implies that if
f\in\mathbb{T}^{S}(K)
acts trivially on
\operatorname{H}^{0}(C^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K})
then its dual
f^{\dagger}
acts trivially on
\operatorname{H}^{0}((C^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K})^{\dagger})\cong\operatorname{H}^{0}(C^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K})
as well. Thus Proposition
(Wt1)
follows immediately from Lemma
10.8
.

We also need the existence of a Hasse invariant in this setting.

Lemma (Wt1)
.

Let
X
be the Shimura curve
X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K}
. There is an element
{\rm Ha}
in
\operatorname{H}^{0}(X_{k},\omega^{\otimes(\ell-1)})
(called a Hasse invariant) which has simple zeros at all the supersingular points of
X_{k}
, and such that the map

\operatorname{H}^{0}(X_{k},\omega^{\otimes k})\to\operatorname{H}^{0}(X_{k},\omega^{\otimes(k+\ell-1)})

given by multiplication by
{\rm Ha}
is equivariant for Hecke operators
T_{r}
and
U_{r}
for all primes
r\neq\ell
and all weights
k\geq 1
, and is also equivariant for
U_{\ell}
if
k\geq 2
.

Further this section lifts to a section
\widetilde{\rm Ha}\in\operatorname{H}^{0}(X_{\mathcal{O}},\omega^{\otimes(\ell-1)})
such that for all
m\geq 1
and all weights
k\geq 1
, the map

\operatorname{H}^{0}(X_{\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{m}},\omega_{\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{m}}^{\otimes k})\to\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(X_{\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{m}},\omega_{\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{m}}^{\otimes\left(k+(\ell-1)\ell^{m-1}\right)}\right)

given by multiplication by
\left(\widetilde{\rm Ha}\right)^{\ell^{m-1}}
is equivariant for Hecke operators
T_{r}
for all
r\not\in S
.

Proof.

The section
{\rm Ha}
exists by
[
15
, Section 5]
. The integral lift is constructed in
[
37
, Section 7]
; this relies on the assumption that
\ell>3
.

The final statement about Hecke equivariance follows by the argument given in
[
11
, Proposition 7.2.1]
. The key point is that while the lift
\widetilde{\rm Ha}\in\operatorname{H}^{0}(X_{\mathcal{O}},\omega^{\otimes(\ell-1)})
is not canonical, the image of
\left(\widetilde{\rm Ha}\right)^{\ell^{m-1}}
in
\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(X_{\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{m}},\omega_{\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{m}}^{\otimes(\ell-1)\ell^{m-1}}\right)
will be independent of the choice of
\widetilde{\rm Ha}
, which allows one to mimic the proof that multiplication by
{\rm Ha}
itself if Hecke equivariant.
∎

14.2.
The conjectures
A
and
B
in weight one

Here is the analog of Conjecture
A
in this setting; we thank George Boxer for explaining the argument to us.

Theorem (Wt1)
.

The following statements hold.

(i)

For every maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}
of
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K)
, there is a semisimple Galois representation
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}\colon G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{T}^{S}(K)/\mathfrak{m})
such that for all
v\not\in S
,
v\neq\ell
,
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{G_{F_{v}}}
is unramified with
\tr\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}(\Frob_{v})=T_{v}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}}
, and
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{G_{\ell}}
is unramified.

(ii)

Furthermore there is a lift of
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}
to a representation

\rho_{K}\colon G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{T}^{S}(K)_{\mathfrak{m}})

such that for all
v\not\in S
,
v\neq\ell
,
\rho_{K}|_{G_{F_{v}}}
is unramified and
\rho_{K}(\Frob_{v})
has characteristic polynomial
x^{2}-T_{v}x+\psi(\Frob_{v})
, where
\psi:G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\mathcal{O}^{\times}
is the Teichmuller lift of
\det\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}
, and
\rho_{K}|_{G_{\ell}}
is unramified.

Proof.

The proof follows the “doubling” strategy used in the proof of
[
16
, Theorem 3.11]
in the case of modular curves. All but one step of proof in loc. cit. works for Shimura curves with no modifications.

Abbreviate
X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K}
by
X
. Take any integer
m\geq 1
. By Lemma
(Wt1)
there exits some power,
A
, of
\widetilde{\rm Ha}
such that the map

\operatorname{H}^{0}(X_{\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{m}},\omega_{\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{m}})\to\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(X_{\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{m}},\omega_{\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{m}}^{\otimes n}\right)

given by multiplication by
A
is equivariant for the Hecke operators
T_{r}
for all
r\not\in S
, where
n-1
is the weight of
A
.

This implies that the “doubling map”

\operatorname{H}^{0}(X,\omega_{E/\mathcal{O}})^{2}[\varpi^{m}]\to\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(X,\omega^{\otimes n}_{E/\mathcal{O}}\right)

given by
(f,g)\mapsto Af+AT_{\ell}(g)-U_{\ell}(Ag)
is also equivariant for all of the Hecke operators for all
r\not\in S
. Here,
T_{\ell}
is the Hecke operator acting on
\operatorname{H}^{0}(X,\omega_{E/\mathcal{O}})
. This can be defined exactly as in
[
10
, Section 3.1]
.
3
3

3

Note that while that argument is written for the modular curve, it does not rely on the
q
-expansion principle, and in fact holds without modification for Shimura curves.

The proof in
[
16
]
reduces (by an argument that still works for Shimura curves) to showing that this doubling map is injective. Moreover, as in loc. cit. it is sufficient to prove this claim in the case
m=1
. Indeed, as
\operatorname{H}^{0}(X,\omega_{E/\mathcal{O}})^{2}[\varpi^{m}]
is a finite length
\mathcal{O}
-module, it suffices to prove that the map

\operatorname{H}^{0}(X,\omega_{k})^{2}=\operatorname{H}^{0}(X,\omega_{E/\mathcal{O}})^{2}[\varpi]\to\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(X,\omega^{\otimes n}_{E/\mathcal{O}}\right)[\varpi]=\operatorname{H}^{0}(X,\omega^{\otimes n}_{k})

is injective. Moreover, as the multiplication by
{\rm Ha}
map

\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(X,\omega^{\otimes k}_{k}\right)\to\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(X,\omega^{\otimes(k+\ell-1)}_{k}\right)

commutes with
U_{\ell}
for
k\geq 2
, it will actually suffice to show that the map

\operatorname{H}^{0}(X,\omega_{k})^{2}\to\operatorname{H}^{0}(X,\omega^{\otimes\ell}_{k})

given by
(f,g)\mapsto{\rm Ha}\cdot f+{\rm Ha}\cdot T_{\ell}(g)-U_{\ell}({\rm Ha}\cdot g)
is injective.

In loc. cit. this is proven via
q
-expansions, which are not available for Shimura curves. However the arguments in
[
9
, Section 5.1]
give an alternative proof of this injectivity which does not rely on
q
-expansions, which completes the proof.

In the specific case of Shimura curves however, one can substantially simplify the proof given in
[
9
, Section 5.1]
. If
(f,g)
is in the kernel of the doubling map, then letting
f_{0}=f+T_{\ell}(g)
, one gets
U_{\ell}({\rm Ha}\cdot g)={\rm Ha}\cdot f_{0}
for
f_{0},g\in\operatorname{H}^{0}(X,\omega_{k})
. As in
[
9
, (5.1.1)]
4
4

4

Again, while
[
9
, §5.1]
only considers weight one forms on a modular curve, the construction of this diagram does not rely on the
q
-expansion principle, and works without modification in the case of Shimura curves
, one can now consider the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}^{0}(X,\omega_{k})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}^{0}(X,\omega^{\otimes\ell}_{k})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}^{0}(SS,\omega_{k})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}^{0}(SS,\omega^{\otimes\ell})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\mapsto U_{\ell}({\rm Ha}\cdot f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

where
SS\subseteq X
is the supersingular locus. Here, the vertical maps are restriction maps and the lower horizontal map is an isomorphism. As
{\rm Ha}
vanishes on
SS
, so does
U_{\ell}({\rm Ha}\cdot g)={\rm Ha}\cdot f_{0}
. Thus
U_{\ell}({\rm Ha}\cdot g)
maps to
0
in
\operatorname{H}^{0}(SS,\omega^{\otimes\ell})
, and so the commutative diagram implies that the restriction of
g
to
SS
must vanish. Thus
g={\rm Ha}\cdot h
for some
g\in\operatorname{H}^{0}(X,\omega^{2-\ell})
. But as
\ell>2
, this cohomology group vanishes, so
g=0
, and hence
(f,g)=(0,0)
.
∎

From now on we fix a Galois representation
\overline{\rho}\colon G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\mathrm{GL}_{2}(k)
for which
\overline{\rho}|_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{\ell})}
is absolutely irreducible and
\overline{\rho}|_{G_{\ell}}
is unramified. Let
N_{\varnothing}=N(\overline{\rho})
denote the conductor of
\overline{\rho}
. We take
\psi\colon G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\mathcal{O}^{\times}
to be the Teichmuller lift of
\det\overline{\rho}
.

We now make more particular choices of the compact open subgroups
K\subseteq D_{\mathfrak{D}}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\infty})
. Let
T
and
Q
be finite sets of primes, disjoint from each other and from
\mathfrak{D}
, satisfying the conditions from Section
12.2
. Let
t
be an auxiliary prime that that is not in
T\cup Q\cup\mathfrak{D}
and satisfies the conditions of Lemma
(Wt1)
above for our choice of
\overline{\rho}
.

As in Section
13.7
, for any integer
N\geq 1
with
(N,\mathfrak{D})=1
we define (identifying
D_{\mathfrak{D}}(\mathbb{Z}_{v})=\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{v})
for
v\not\in\mathfrak{D}
):

\displaystyle K_{0}^{\mathfrak{D}}(N)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in D_{\mathfrak{D}}^{\times}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}})\middle|c\equiv 0\pmod{Nt^{2}},d\equiv 1\pmod{t^{2}}\right\}\subseteq D_{\mathfrak{D}}^{\times}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}})

\displaystyle K_{1}^{\mathfrak{D}}(N)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in D_{\mathfrak{D}}^{\times}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}})\middle|c\equiv 0\pmod{Nt^{2}},d\equiv 1\pmod{Nt^{2}}\right\}\subseteq D_{\mathfrak{D}}^{\times}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}})

so that again
K_{1}^{\mathfrak{D}}(N)\unlhd K_{0}^{\mathfrak{D}}(N)
with
K_{0}^{\mathfrak{D}}(N)/K_{1}^{\mathfrak{D}}(N)\cong(\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z})^{\times}
, and
K_{0}^{\mathfrak{D}}(N)
and
K_{1}^{\mathfrak{D}}(N)
are sufficiently small. Also define
K^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Delta}(N)
and
K^{\mathfrak{D}}_{H}(N)
to be the smallest intermediate subgroups

K^{\mathfrak{D}}_{1}(N)\leq K^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Delta}(N),K^{\mathfrak{D}}_{H}(N)\leq K^{\mathfrak{D}}_{0}(N)

for which
|K^{\mathfrak{D}}_{0}(N)/K^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Delta}(N)|
is an
\ell^{th}
power and
|K^{\mathfrak{D}}_{0}(N)/K^{\mathfrak{D}}_{H}(N)|
is prime to
\ell
. For any
M
and
N
define
K_{H,\Delta}^{\mathfrak{D}}(N,M)=K_{H}^{\mathfrak{D}}(N)\cap K^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Delta}(M)
.

For any subset
\Sigma\subseteq T
, set
N_{\Sigma}=N_{\phi}\Sigma
, where by
\Sigma
we mean the product of the primes in
\Sigma
.

For convenience, write
K_{\Sigma,Q}^{\mathfrak{D}}=K_{H,\Delta}^{\mathfrak{D}}(N_{\Sigma},Q)
, and
K_{\Sigma}^{\mathfrak{D}}=K_{\Sigma,\varnothing}^{\mathfrak{D}}=K_{H}^{\mathfrak{D}}(N_{\Sigma})
. Let
X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma,Q}=X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma,Q}}
,
\rho^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma,Q}=\rho_{K^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma,Q}}
,
X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma}=X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma,\varnothing}
and
\rho^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma}=\rho^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma,\varnothing}
.

By Proposition
(Wt1)
,
C^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K_{\Sigma,Q}^{\mathfrak{D}}}
can be given the structure of a perfect complex of
\mathcal{O}[\Delta_{Q}]
-modules. By construction, the action of
\mathcal{O}[\Delta_{Q}]
defined by this structure coincides with the action of
\mathcal{O}[\Delta_{Q}]
on
C^{\mathfrak{D}}_{K_{\Sigma,Q}^{\mathfrak{D}}}
defined using the diamond operators
\langle d\rangle_{v}
for
v\in Q
.

Let
S
be a finite set of primes containing
T\cup Q\cup\mathfrak{D}
, all primes dividing
N_{\varnothing}
and the prime
t
. From now on we assume that
\overline{\rho}
arises (in the sense of Theorem
(Wt1)
) from a maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}
of the Hecke algebra
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\varnothing})
acting on
\operatorname{H}^{1}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\varnothing},\omega_{\mathcal{O}})
(which is isomorphic to
\operatorname{H}^{0}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\varnothing},\omega_{E/\mathcal{O}})^{*}
).

Let
\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma,Q}
be the localization
\mathbb{T}^{S}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma,Q})_{\mathfrak{m}}
. We observe now that the analog of Conjecture
B
can be proved in this setting by the variant of doubling method used in the proof of Theorem
(Wt1)
.

Theorem (Wt1)
.

For any
\Sigma
,
Q
and
\mathfrak{D}
, the representations
\rho^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma}\colon G_{F}\to\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma})
stemming from Theorem
(Wt1)
arise from the corresponding universal representation
G_{F}\to\mathrm{GL}_{2}(R^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma,Q})
via maps
R^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma,Q}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma,Q}
and satisfy the following properties:

(1)

\tr\rho^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma,Q}(\Frob_{v})=T_{v}
for all
v\not\in S
.

(2)

There is a natural
\mathcal{O}[\Delta_{Q}]
-algebra structure on
\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma,Q}
defined analogously to as in Section
13
(via the full Hecke algebra) which makes the map
R^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma,Q}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma,Q}
into a
\mathcal{O}[\Delta_{Q}]
-algebra homomorphism.

(3)

For
v\in\Sigma
, writing
B_{v}\in\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}^{\mathfrak{D},\square}
for the image of
B_{v}\in R_{v}^{\square}
under the map

R_{v}^{\square}\hookrightarrow R_{\Sigma,\loc}\to R_{\Sigma}^{\square}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}^{\mathfrak{D},\square}

we have
(B_{v})=(\epsilon_{v}\sqrt{q_{v}\psi(\varphi_{v})^{-1}}U_{v}-1)
as ideals of
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}^{\mathfrak{D},\square}
.

(4)

For
\Sigma_{1}\subseteq\Sigma_{2}\subseteq T
, the diagram below commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{\Sigma,Q}^{\mathfrak{D}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q}^{\mathfrak{D}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{\Sigma}^{\mathfrak{D}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}^{\mathfrak{D}}}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{\Sigma_{2},Q}^{\mathfrak{D}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma_{2},Q}^{\mathfrak{D}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{\Sigma_{1},Q}^{\mathfrak{D}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma_{1},Q}^{\mathfrak{D}}}

For any
\Sigma\subseteq T
,
Q
and
v\in T\smallsetminus\Sigma
, we can now define the complex
C_{\Sigma,Q}^{\mathfrak{D}}\in{\mathsf{D}}_{\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q}}(\mathcal{O})
and the level lowering map
\pi_{v}\colon C_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},Q}^{\mathfrak{D}}\to C_{\Sigma,Q}^{\mathfrak{D}}
completely analogously to the definition given in Section
13
(that is, by localizing at an appropriate maximal ideal of an extension of
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q}^{\mathfrak{D}}
).

Note that
C_{\Sigma,Q}^{\mathfrak{D}}
again has the structure of a perfect complex of
\mathcal{O}[\Delta_{Q}]
-modules, and we have
C_{\Sigma,Q}^{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{\mathcal{O}[\Delta_{Q}]}\mathcal{O}\cong C_{\Sigma}^{\mathfrak{D}}
in
{\mathsf{D}}_{\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}}(\mathcal{O})
.

Proposition (Wt1)
.

There is a family of isomorphisms

\varphi_{\Sigma,Q}^{\mathfrak{D}}\colon C_{\Sigma,Q}^{\mathfrak{D}}\xrightarrow{\sim}{(C_{\Sigma,Q}^{\mathfrak{D}})}^{\dagger}

in
{\mathsf{D}}_{\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q}}(\mathcal{O})
compatible with the isomorphisms
C_{\Sigma,Q}^{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{\mathcal{O}[\Delta_{Q}]}\mathcal{O}\cong C_{\Sigma}^{\mathfrak{D}}
.

Moreover for each
v\in T\smallsetminus\Sigma
, if

\pi_{v}^{\dagger}\colon C_{\Sigma,Q}^{\mathfrak{D}}\cong{(C_{\Sigma,Q}^{\mathfrak{D}})}^{\dagger}\to{(C_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},Q}^{\mathfrak{D}})}^{\dagger}\cong C_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},Q}^{\mathfrak{D}}

is the adjoint of
\pi_{v}\colon C_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},Q}^{\mathfrak{D}}\to C_{\Sigma,Q}^{\mathfrak{D}}
upon using the isomorphisms
\varphi_{\Sigma,Q}
and
\varphi_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},Q}
, then one has

\displaystyle\pi_{v}\circ\pi_{v}^{\dagger}\simeq q_{v}U_{v}^{2}-\psi(\varphi_{v})\in\operatorname{End}_{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O})}(C_{\Sigma,Q}^{\mathfrak{D}})

\displaystyle\pi_{v}^{\dagger}\circ\pi_{v}\simeq q_{v}U_{v}^{2}-\psi(\varphi_{v})\in\operatorname{End}_{\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{O})}(C_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},Q}^{\mathfrak{D}}),

where
\simeq
means up to units in the corresponding Hecke algebras.

Proof.

Using the arguments of
[
10
, Section 3.2]
, we deduce the existence of isomorphisms

\varphi^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma,Q}\colon C^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma,Q}\xrightarrow{\sim}{(C^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma,Q})}^{\dagger}

using Serre duality applied to the sheaves
\omega_{A}
and using the Kodaira-Spencer isomorphism that gives
\omega_{A}^{\otimes 2}=\Omega_{A}
where
\Omega_{A}
is the canonical sheaf on the Shimura curve
X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma,Q}
. After this the Proposition
(Wt1)
follows from arguments analogous to the ones given in Section
13
(see proof of Proposition
(Wt1)
).
∎

14.3.
The conjectures
C
and
D
in weight one

For any curve
X
, the cohomology groups
\operatorname{H}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F})
with coefficients in a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}
are non-zero only for
i=0,1
. It follows from the definition of
C_{K}^{\mathfrak{D}}
that we deduce that
\operatorname{H}_{i}(C_{K}^{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{\mathcal{O}}k)=0
for
i\not\in[0,1]
. Thus the analogue of Conjecture
C
holds in this setting.

The analogue of conjecture
D
follows from the following proposition:

Proposition (Wt1)
.

The map

\operatorname{H}^{0}(\pi_{v})\colon\operatorname{H}^{0}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma},\omega_{E/\mathcal{O}})^{*}_{\mathfrak{m}_{\Sigma}}\to\operatorname{H}^{0}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\{v\}},\omega_{E/\mathcal{O}})^{*}_{\mathfrak{m}_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\{v\}}}

for
v\in\Sigma
is surjective.

Proof.

By duality it suffices to prove that the kernel of the map
\operatorname{H}^{0}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\{v\}},\omega_{k})^{2}\to\operatorname{H}^{0}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma},\omega_{k})
is Eisenstein (and in particular vanishes on localizing at
\mathfrak{m}_{\Sigma}
). This follows from
[
24
, Proposition 5]
.
∎

14.4.
R=\mathbb{T}
theorems and the torsion Jacquet–Langlands correspondence in the weight one case

In Section
15
we prove the following Theorem (as a consequence of Theorem
(Wt1)
in Case
(Wt1)
):

Theorem (Wt1)
.

The surjective maps
R^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma}\to\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma}
are isomorphisms.

As a corollary of this, we deduce a torsion Jacquet–Langlands correspondence for weight one modular forms.

Theorem (Wt1)
.

Consider a residual representation
\overline{\rho}:G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\mathrm{GL}_{2}(k)
that arises from a maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{D}}
of the Hecke algebra acting on
\operatorname{H}^{1}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma},\omega_{\mathcal{O}})
. We assume that
\overline{\rho}|_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{\ell})}
is irreducible. Then:

(1)

\overline{\rho}
also arises from a maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}_{\varnothing}
of the Hecke algebra acting on
\operatorname{H}^{1}(X^{\varnothing}_{\Sigma\cup\mathfrak{D}},\omega_{\mathcal{O}})
.

(2)

For any set
\Sigma
of level raising primes satisfying the conditions from Section
12.2
, if
\mathbb{T}^{\varnothing}_{\Sigma\cup\mathfrak{D}}
and
\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma}
are the Hecke algebras acting on
\operatorname{H}^{1}(X^{\varnothing}_{\Sigma\cup\mathfrak{D}},\omega_{\mathcal{O}})
and
\operatorname{H}^{1}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma},\omega_{\mathcal{O}})
, localized at
\mathfrak{m}_{\varnothing}
and
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{D}}
respectively, then there is a natural surjective map
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma\cup\mathfrak{D}}^{\varnothing}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}^{\mathfrak{D}}
with kernel generated by
q_{v}U_{v}^{2}-\psi(\Frob_{v})
for
v\in\mathfrak{D}
.

Proof.

We first observe that by using the duality
\operatorname{H}^{0}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma},\omega_{E/\mathcal{O}})^{*}=\operatorname{H}^{1}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma},\omega_{\mathcal{O}})
, and that
\operatorname{H}^{0}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma},\omega_{k})=\operatorname{H}^{0}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma},\omega_{E/\mathcal{O}})[\varpi]
, we deduce that

\overline{\rho}
arises from
\operatorname{H}^{0}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma},\omega_{k})
. By multiplying by the Hasse invariant we then get that
\overline{\rho}
also arises from
\operatorname{H}^{0}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma},\omega_{k}^{\otimes\ell})
.

Let
X=X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma}
. Consider the long exact sequence of cohomology arising from the exact sequence of sheaves on
X
:

0\rightarrow\omega_{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes{\ell}}\rightarrow\omega_{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes{\ell}}\rightarrow\omega_{k}^{\otimes{\ell}}\rightarrow 0

(where the second arrow is multiplication by a uniformizer
\varpi
of
\mathcal{O}
). We claim that
\operatorname{H}^{1}(X_{\mathcal{O}},\omega_{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes{\ell}})[\varpi]=0
. Indeed
\operatorname{H}^{1}(X_{\mathcal{O}},\omega_{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes{\ell}})
vanishes as using Serre duality, and the Kodaira-Spencer isomorphism
\omega^{\otimes 2}\simeq\Omega_{X}
, this follows from the vanishing of
\operatorname{H}^{0}(X_{k},\omega_{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes{(2-\ell)}})
(for
\ell>2
).

From the claim, and the fact that
\overline{\rho}
arises from
\operatorname{H}^{0}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\varnothing},\omega_{k}^{\otimes\ell})
, we deduce that
\overline{\rho}
arises from a classical weight
\ell
form
f
that is a section of
\operatorname{H}^{0}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\varnothing},\omega_{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes\ell})
.

To prove the existence of
\mathfrak{m}_{\varnothing}
, we can now apply the classical Jacquet–Langlands correspondence to
f
and get a classical weight
\ell
form
f_{\varnothing}
on
X^{\varnothing}_{\mathfrak{D}}
with residual representation
\overline{\rho}
. One can then apply level and weight optimization on the modular curve to get the desired maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}_{\varnothing}
producing
\overline{\rho}
. Here one uses crucially the companion forms results of
[
27
]
and
[
21
]
to lower the weight from
\ell
to 1 using that the mod
\ell
representation
\overline{\rho}
that
f_{\varnothing}
gives rise to is unramified at
\ell
. This proves (1).

To prove (2), using Theorem
(Wt1)
(for
\mathfrak{D}\neq\varnothing
) and the results in
[
18
]
(for
\mathfrak{D}=\varnothing
) and part (1), we deduce that for any set
\Sigma
of level raising primes for
\overline{\rho}
that satisfy our earlier conditions, we have isomorphisms
R^{\varnothing}_{\Sigma\cup\mathfrak{D}}\cong\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma\cup\mathfrak{D}}^{\varnothing}
and
R^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma}\cong\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}^{\mathfrak{D}}
, so the claim follows from the analogous statement for the map
R^{\varnothing}_{\Sigma\cup\mathfrak{D}}\to R^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma}
, which is clear from the definitions.
∎

Remark (Wt1)
.

If one had available results about companion forms for mod
\ell
representations on Shimura curves one could show that if
\overline{\rho}
arises from
\operatorname{H}^{1}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma},\omega_{\mathcal{O}})
then it also arises from
\operatorname{H}^{1}(X^{\mathfrak{D}’}_{{\Sigma}\cup{\mathfrak{D}}\backslash{\mathfrak{D}’}},\omega_{\mathcal{O}})
for all
\mathfrak{D}’\subseteq\mathfrak{D}
, and not just
\mathfrak{D}’=\varnothing
. This will need results for companion forms on Shimura curves (to lower the weight from
\ell
to 1 when the mod
\ell
representation is unramified at
\ell
) that would be the analogs of the results of Gross and Coleman–Voloch
[
27
]
and
[
21
]
on modular curves. This also is the obstruction to going from a maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}_{\Sigma}
of the Hecke algebra acting on
\operatorname{H}^{1}(X^{\varnothing}_{\Sigma\cup\mathfrak{D}},\omega_{\mathcal{O}})
to a maximal ideal

\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{D}}
of the Hecke algebra acting on
\operatorname{H}^{1}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma},\omega_{\mathcal{O}})
(under the necessary assumption that the primes in
\mathfrak{D}
are level raising primes for
\overline{\rho}
arising from
\mathfrak{m}_{\varnothing}
). Toby Gee has informed us that the possibility of proving a result like Theorem
(Wt1)
had been considered earlier by him in work with Boxer and Calegari.

15.
Applications to integral modularity lifting

In this section we apply the results from Part
I
in order to prove
R=\mathbb{T}
theorems in non-minimal cases. The essential strategy is the same as the arguments given in
[
56
, Chapter 2]
and
[
25
, Theorem 3.4]
. Namely, one first proves the result in the minimal level case via patching — in our case the relevant patching result is already known, see
[
16
, Theorem 1.3]
. One then proceeds by induction on the level, using Ihara’s Lemma to control the growth of congruence modules, and then using the numerical criterion to prove the isomorphism
R_{\Sigma}=\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}
.

The key difference in our approach is that we apply the inductive argument and the numerical criterion directly to the patched modules
M_{\Sigma,\infty}
, rather than applying them to the global deformation rings and Hecke algebras. This allows us to sidestep many of the complications that arise by working over non totally real fields or with weight
1
modular forms (the “
\ell_{0}>0
” case) as the patched situation in these cases behaves quite similarly to that for weight
2
modular forms over
\mathbb{Q}
. This modification to the argument is what necessitates working with congruence modules in higher codimension.

In both minimal and non-minimal cases the patched modules
M_{\Sigma,\infty}
are known to be maximal Cohen–Macaulay over the corresponding patched deformation rings
R_{\Sigma,\infty}
. In the minimal case (i.e.
\Sigma=\varnothing
)
R_{\varnothing,\infty}
is a power series ring, which is well-known to imply
M_{\varnothing,\infty}
is free, giving our base case. However in the non-minimal case our patched rings
R_{\Sigma,\infty}
are not power series rings, and have multiple components (although they are quite nice and easy to describe, and in particular are complete intersections in our case). This causes difficulty in analyzing the structure of patched modules in the non-minimal case purely via patching.

We show by induction on
\Sigma
, using our version of the numerical criterion, Theorem
9.6
(although for our purposes Theorem
9.2
is sufficient, as we already know that relevant local deformation rings are complete intersections), that each
M_{\Sigma,\infty}
has a free direct summand. The desired statement about global deformation rings and Hecke algebras (Theorem
(Wt1)
) follows immediately from this.

Proposition
4.4
, together with Theorem
7.4
, provides the necessary generalization of the growth of congruence modules argument of
loc. cit.
, provided one assumes the version of Ihara’s Lemma given in Conjecture
D
(which is known in the case of Bianchi manifolds by Theorem
(Wt1)
) or Proposition
(Wt1)
.

One should note that the augmentation
\lambda\colon R_{\Sigma,\infty}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{O}
which we define below in order to apply our numerical criterion is picked essentially arbitrarily, and need have no relation to the global deformation rings and Hecke algebras. This means in particular that our method does not need to distinguish between the cases where the ring
\mathbb{T}
has characteristic
0
points and the case when it does not.

One slight complication that arises in our argument, compared to that of
loc. cit.
, is that we have no direct control over the generic rank of the patched modules
M_{\Sigma,\infty}
, unless the Hecke algebra
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}
has at least one characteristic
0
point lying on each component of
\operatorname{Spec}R_{\Sigma,\infty}
. Fortunately Theorem
9.2
needs no assumption about the generic rank of the module (in contrast to
[
25
, Theorem 2.4]
), so this does not present an issue for our main application to proving
R=\mathbb{T}
. This does however prevent us from proving an analogue of the ‘multiplicity one’ result of
[
25
, Theorem 3.4]
—instead we can only prove the weaker statement in Theorem
(Wt1)
. Note that this issue is already present in
[
16
, Theorem 1.3]
, although it is somewhat more severe in our case as
\operatorname{Spec}R_{\Sigma,\infty}
has multiple components and one cannot conclude that
M_{\Sigma,\infty}
has the same generic rank on each component.

We treat the two cases
(PGL2)
and
(Wt1)
in parallel here. To fix notation:

•

In case
(PGL2)
we freely use all notation introduced in Section
13
, and assume conjectures
A
,
B
,
C
and
D
. In particular we consider a non-Eisenstein maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}\subseteq\mathbb{T}^{S}(K_{0}(\mathcal{N}_{\varnothing}))
with residue field
k
, and the corresponding Galois representation
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}\colon G_{F}\to\mathrm{GL}_{2}(k)
. We assume that
\mathfrak{m}
was chosen so that
N(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}})=\mathcal{N}_{\varnothing}
.

For each
\Sigma\subseteq T
we then have rings
R_{\Sigma,Q}
and
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q}
, a surjective map
R_{\Sigma,Q}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q}
from Conjecture
B
and a complex
C_{\Sigma,Q}\in{\mathsf{D}}_{\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q}}(\mathcal{O})
. These will again be denoted
R_{\Sigma}
,
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}
and
C_{\Sigma}
when
Q=\varnothing
.

We again let
r_{1}
and
r_{2}
be the number of real and complex places of
F
respectively, and let
d=r_{1}+r_{2}
and
l_{0}=r_{2}
. Note that by Conjecture
C
,
\operatorname{H}_{i}(k\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{\mathcal{O}}C_{\Sigma})=0
for
i\not\in[d,d+\ell_{0}]
and
\operatorname{H}_{d}(C_{\Sigma})=\operatorname{H}_{r_{1}+r_{2}}(Y_{0}(\mathcal{N}_{\Sigma}),\mathcal{O})_{\mathfrak{m}_{\Sigma}}
.

•

In case
(Wt1)
we freely use all notation introduced in Section
14
. Again, we consider a non-Eisenstein maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}\subseteq\mathbb{T}^{S}(K^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\varnothing})
with residue field
k
, and the corresponding Galois representation
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}\colon G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\mathrm{GL}_{2}(k)
. We assume that
\mathfrak{m}
was chosen so that
N(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}})=N_{\varnothing}
.

The discriminant
\mathfrak{D}
is fixed throughout this section, and so omit it from our notation.
Thus we consider rings
R_{\Sigma,Q}
and
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q}
, a surjective map
R_{\Sigma,Q}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q}
and a complex
C_{\Sigma,Q}\in{\mathsf{D}}_{\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q}}(\mathcal{O})
. These will again be denoted
R_{\Sigma}
,
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}
and
C_{\Sigma}
when
Q=\varnothing
.

Here we let
d=0
and
l_{0}=1
. Then we again have
\operatorname{H}_{i}(k\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{\mathcal{O}}C_{\Sigma})=0
for
i\not\in[d,d+\ell_{0}]
and
\operatorname{H}_{d}(C_{\Sigma})=\operatorname{H}^{0}(X^{\mathfrak{D}}_{\Sigma},\omega_{E/\mathcal{O}})^{*}_{\mathfrak{m}_{\Sigma}}
.

Our main result is the following, which is Theorem
F
in Case
(PGL2)
and Theorem
H
in Case
(Wt1)
:

Theorem (Wt1)
.

Assume that
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{G_{F(\zeta_{\ell})}}
is absolutely irreducible. Then there exists an integer
\mu\geq 1
such that for each
\Sigma\subseteq T
there exists an
R_{\Sigma}
-module
W_{\Sigma}
and an isomorphism of
R_{\Sigma}
-modules

\operatorname{H}_{d}(C_{\Sigma})\cong R_{\Sigma}^{\mu}\oplus W_{\Sigma}\,.

In particular,
R_{\Sigma}
acts faithfully on
\operatorname{H}_{d}(C_{\Sigma})
and so the map
R_{\Sigma}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}
is an isomorphism for all
\Sigma
.

This result is deduced from Theorem
(Wt1)
below.
As in Section
12.2
, let
S
be the set of all finite places of
F
where either
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{p}}|_{G_{F_{v}}}
ramifies or
v|\ell
. Let
j=4|S\cup T\cup\mathfrak{D}|-1
, so that for all
\Sigma
and
Q
one has

R_{\Sigma}^{\square}=R_{\Sigma}[\![{w_{1},\ldots,w_{j}}]\!]\quad\text{and}\quad R_{\Sigma,Q}^{\square}=R_{\Sigma,Q}[\![{w_{1},\ldots,w_{j}}]\!]\,.

The result below that is by now standard (see for instance
[
16
, Proposition 5.2]
) allows us to construct sets of Taylor–Wiles primes
Q_{n}
:

Proposition (Wt1)
.

Assume that
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{F(\zeta_{\ell})}
is absolutely irreducible. Then there exist integers
r,g\geq 1
such that for all integers
n\geq 1
there is a set of finite places
Q_{n}
of
F
for which:

•

|Q_{n}|=r
;

•

Q_{n}\cap(S\cup T\cup\mathfrak{D})=\varnothing
;

•

\Nm(v)\equiv 1\pmod{\ell^{n}}
for each
v\in Q_{n}
;

•

\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}
is unramified at
v
and
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}(\Frob_{v})
has distinct eigenvalues in
k
for each
v\in Q_{n}
;

•

The map
R_{\loc,T}\to R_{T,Q_{n}}^{\square}
extends to a surjection

R_{\loc,T}[\![{x_{1},\ldots,x_{g}}]\!]\twoheadrightarrow R_{T,Q_{n}}^{\square}

thus inducing compatible surjections
R_{\loc,\Sigma}[\![{x_{1},\ldots,x_{g}}]\!]\twoheadrightarrow R_{\Sigma,Q_{n}}^{\square}
for
\Sigma\subseteq T
.

Moreover we have

\dim R_{T,\loc}[\![{x_{1},\ldots,x_{g}}]\!]=\dim R_{T,\loc}+g=(1+j+r)-l_{0}\,.

Now for each
\Sigma\subseteq T
let
R_{\Sigma,\infty}=R_{\Sigma,\loc}[\![{x_{1},\ldots,x_{g}}]\!]
. Also let

S_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}[\![{{\boldsymbol{y}},{\boldsymbol{w}}}]\!]=\mathcal{O}[\![{y_{1},\ldots,y_{r},w_{1},\ldots,w_{j}}]\!]

so that
\dim R_{\Sigma,\infty}=\dim S_{\infty}-l_{0}
for all
\Sigma
. For
v\in\Sigma
, let
A_{v},B_{v}\in R_{v}^{{\rm uni}(\epsilon_{v})}\subseteq R_{\Sigma,\infty}
be as in Proposition
(Wt1)
, so that
R_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},\infty}/(A_{v})=R_{\Sigma,\infty}
.

Write
c=j+r-l_{0}
, so that
\dim R_{\Sigma,\infty}=c+1
for all
\Sigma
.

For any
n\geq 1
, let
\Lambda_{n}=\mathcal{O}[\![{\Delta_{Q_{n}}}]\!]
. Since
|Q_{n}|=r
,
\Delta_{Q_{n}}
is isomorphic to
\prod_{i=1}^{r}(\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{e(i,n)}\mathbb{Z})
for some integers
e(i,n)\geq n
. Thus we may write
\Lambda_{n}=\mathcal{O}[\![{{\boldsymbol{y}}}]\!]/I_{n}
for some ideal
I_{n}\subseteq\mathcal{O}[\![{{\boldsymbol{y}}}]\!]
as in Section
10.1
.

Now let
R_{\Sigma,n}=R_{\Sigma,Q_{n}}
and
C_{\Sigma,n}=C_{\Sigma,Q_{n}}
. Also let
R_{\Sigma,0}=R_{\Sigma}
and
C_{\Sigma,0}=C_{\Sigma}
. Note that we may regard
C_{\Sigma,n}
as an element of
{\mathsf{D}}_{R_{\Sigma,n}}(\Lambda_{n})
via the composition
R_{\Sigma,n}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma,Q_{n}}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{End}_{\mathsf{D}(\Lambda_{n})}(C_{\Sigma,n})
. Let
\alpha_{\Sigma,n}
be the isomorphism

C_{\Sigma,n}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{\Lambda_{n}}\mathcal{O}\xrightarrow{\sim}C_{\Sigma,0}

from Proposition
(Wt1)
.
Recalling that
\operatorname{H}_{*}(k\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{\Lambda_{n}}C_{\Sigma,n})
vanishes outside of
[d,d+\ell_{0}]
, the work of Section
13
(Propositions
(Wt1)
,
(Wt1)
,
(Wt1)
) in Case
(PGL2)
and Section
14
(Proposition
(Wt1)
) in Case
(Wt1)
gives the following:

Lemma (Wt1)
.

For each
\Sigma\subseteq T
the tuple
{\mathfrak{C}}_{\Sigma}=\left(C_{\Sigma,n},\alpha_{\Sigma,n}\right)_{n\geqslant 0}
is a patching system. Moreover there is an isomorphism
{\mathfrak{C}}_{\Sigma}^{\vee}\cong{\mathfrak{C}}_{\Sigma}
of patching systems. ∎

Now for any
\Sigma\subseteq T
let
M_{\Sigma,\infty}=\mathcal{P}({\mathfrak{C}}_{\Sigma})
. By Theorems
10.6
and
11.3
each
M_{\Sigma,\infty}
is a self-dual maximal Cohen–Macaulay
R_{T,\infty}
-module. Also by construction the action of
R_{T,\infty}
on
