M_{\Sigma,\infty}
factors through
R_{\Sigma,\infty}
.

Moreover (after picking an appoperiate homomorphism
S_{\infty}\to R_{T,\infty}
as in Theorem
10.6
) we have
R_{\Sigma,\infty}\otimes_{S_{\infty}}\mathcal{O}\cong R_{\Sigma}
and
M_{\Sigma,\infty}\otimes_{S_{\infty}}\mathcal{O}\cong\operatorname{H}_{d}(C_{\Sigma})
.

Theorem
(Wt1)
will thus follow from the following:

Theorem (Wt1)
.

There is an integer
\mu\geq 1
such that for each
\Sigma\subseteq T
there is an isomorphism
M_{\Sigma,\infty}\cong R_{\Sigma,\infty}^{\mu}\oplus W_{\Sigma,\infty}
for some
R_{\Sigma,\infty}
-module
W_{\Sigma,\infty}
.

We need the following consequence of Theorem
11.3
, Proposition
(Wt1)
and Conjecture
D
(in particular, its consequence Lemma
(Wt1)
) in Case
(PGL2)
, and Propositions
(Wt1)
and
(Wt1)
in Case
(Wt1)
.

Proposition (Wt1)
.

For each
\Sigma\subseteq T
and each
v\in T\smallsetminus\Sigma
the family of maps
\pi_{v}\colon C_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},n}\to C_{\Sigma,n}
induces a morphism
{\mathfrak{C}}_{\Sigma\cup\{v\}}\to{\mathfrak{C}}_{\Sigma}
and hence a
R_{T,\infty}
-module homomorphism
\pi_{v}\colon M_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},\infty}\to M_{\Sigma,\infty}
.

Moreover the map
\pi_{v}\colon M_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},\infty}\to M_{\Sigma,\infty}
is surjective, and if
\pi_{v}^{\vee}\colon M_{\Sigma,\infty}\to M_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},\infty}
is the dual map, induced by the self-duality of
M_{\Sigma,\infty}
and
M_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},\infty}
, then for some units
u_{v},u^{\prime}_{v}\in R_{T,\infty}^{\times}
we have

\pi_{v}\circ\pi_{v}^{\vee}=u_{v}B_{v}\in R_{T,\infty}\quad\text{and}\quad\pi_{v}^{\vee}\circ\pi_{v}=u^{\prime}_{v}B_{v}\in R_{T,\infty}\,.

Proof.

The surjectivity of
\pi_{v}
follows from Lemma
(Wt1)
and Nakayama’s Lemma. Next we note that under the map

R_{v}^{{\rm uni}(\epsilon_{v})}\hookrightarrow R_{T,\infty}\twoheadrightarrow R_{\Sigma}^{\square}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}^{\square}\,,

the element
B_{v}
maps to
w_{v}(\epsilon_{v}\sqrt{q_{v}\psi(\varphi_{v})^{-1}}U_{v}-1)
for some unit
w_{v}\in(\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}^{\square})^{\times}
by Conjecture
B
(3) and Theorem
(Wt1)
(3) which means that
w_{v}^{-1}B_{v}(2+w_{v}^{-1}B_{v})
maps to

\displaystyle(\epsilon_{v}\sqrt{q_{v}\psi(\varphi_{v})^{-1}}U_{v}-1)(\epsilon_{v}\sqrt{q_{v}\psi(\varphi_{v})^{-1}}U_{v}+1)

\displaystyle=(\epsilon_{v}\sqrt{q_{v}\psi(\varphi_{v})^{-1}}U_{v})^{2}-1

\displaystyle=q_{v}\psi(\varphi_{v})^{-1}U_{v}^{2}-1

(as
\epsilon_{v}=\pm 1
). Recall that in Case
(PGL2)
this equals
U_{v}^{2}-1
since
\psi(\varphi_{v})=q_{v}
.

Note that
\psi(\varphi_{v})\in\mathcal{O}^{\times}
and
2+w_{v}^{-1}B_{v}\in R_{T,\infty}^{\times}
, as
B_{v}\in\mathfrak{m}_{R_{T,\infty}}
and
2\in\mathcal{O}^{\times}
.
Using the formulas for maps
\pi_{v}\circ\pi_{v}^{\dagger}
and
\pi_{v}^{\dagger}\circ\pi_{v}
of the complexes
{\mathfrak{C}}_{\Sigma},{\mathfrak{C}}_{\Sigma\cup\{v\}}
in Proposition
(Wt1)
in Case
(PGL2)
and Proposition
(Wt1)
in Case
(Wt1)
, and Theorem
11.3
, which gives that
\mathcal{P}(\pi_{v}^{\dagger})=\mathcal{P}(\pi_{v})^{\vee}
, we deduce the formulas for
\pi_{v}\circ\pi_{v}^{\vee}
and
\pi_{v}^{\vee}\circ\pi_{v}
.
∎

Now pick an augmentation
\lambda\colon R_{T,\infty}\twoheadrightarrow R_{\varnothing,\infty}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{O}
such that
\lambda(A_{v})=0
and
\lambda(B_{v})\neq 0
for all
v\in T
. It follows that each
R_{\Sigma,\infty}
is regular at
\mathfrak{p}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{Ker}\lambda
, and so
(R_{\Sigma,\infty},\lambda)\in\operatorname{C}_{\mathcal{O}}(c)
. Let
\mu=\operatorname{rank}_{\mathfrak{p}}M_{\varnothing,\infty}
, and note that
M_{\varnothing,\infty}\cong R_{\varnothing,\infty}^{\mu}
, by the Auslander–Buchsbaum formula. (Note that
M_{\varnothing,\infty}\neq 0
as
C_{\varnothing}\neq 0
by the choice of
\mathfrak{m}
, and so
\mu\geq 1
.)

Lemma (Wt1)
.

For each
\Sigma\subseteq T
and each
v\in T\smallsetminus\Sigma
the map

\pi_{v}\colon M_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},\infty}\longrightarrow M_{\Sigma,\infty}

is an isomorphism at
\mathfrak{p}
. In particular,
\operatorname{rank}_{\mathfrak{p}}M_{\Sigma,\infty}=\mu
for all
\Sigma
.

Proof.

By the choice of
\lambda
,
B_{v}\not\in\mathfrak{p}
, and so
B_{v}
is a unit in
(R_{T,\infty})_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Thus by Proposition
(Wt1)
, the maps
\pi_{v}\circ\pi_{v}^{\vee}=u_{v}B_{v}
and
\pi_{v}^{\vee}\circ\pi_{v}=u^{\prime}_{v}B_{v}
are both multiplication by units in
(R_{T,\infty})_{\mathfrak{p}}
, after localizing at
\mathfrak{p}
. The claim follows.
∎

Remark (Wt1)
.

To prove the lemma it suffices to have an element
\alpha_{v}\in R_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},\infty}
which annihilates
\operatorname{Ker}\pi_{v}
and maps to an element of
R_{\varnothing,\infty}
that is not in
\operatorname{Ker}\lambda
; the computation
\pi_{v}^{\vee}\circ\pi_{v}=u^{\prime}_{v}B_{v}
shows that we may take
\alpha_{v}=B_{v}
. On the other hand for the proof of Theorem
(Wt1)
below it is crucial to have the precise computation that
(\pi_{v}\circ\pi_{v}^{\vee})=(B_{v})
as ideals of
R_{\Sigma,\infty}
to compute the change of congruence modules
\operatorname{length}_{\mathcal{O}}\Psi_{R_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},\infty}}(M_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},\infty})=\operatorname{length}_{\mathcal{O}}\Psi_{R_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},\infty}}(M_{\Sigma,\infty})+\mu\cdot\nu_{\mathcal{O}}(\lambda(B_{v}))
.

Proof of Theorem
(Wt1)
.

For any
\Sigma\subseteq T
and
v\in T\smallsetminus\Sigma
, as
\pi_{v}\circ\pi_{v}^{\vee}=u_{v}B_{v}
we get using Proposition
(Wt1)
that the composition

\displaystyle F_{R_{T,\infty}}^{c}(M_{\Sigma,\infty})

\displaystyle\cong F_{R_{T,\infty}}^{c}(M_{\Sigma,\infty}^{\vee})\xrightarrow{\pi_{v}^{\vee}}F_{R_{T,\infty}}^{c}(M_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},\infty}^{\vee})\cong F_{R_{T,\infty}}^{c}(M_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},\infty})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\pi_{v}}F_{R_{T,\infty}}^{c}(M_{\Sigma,\infty})

is multiplication by
\lambda(u_{v}B_{v})
and so as
F_{R_{T,\infty}}^{c}(M_{\Sigma,\infty})\cong\mathcal{O}^{\mu}
, its determinant is
\lambda(u_{v})^{\mu}\lambda(B_{v})^{\mu}
. As
u_{v}
is a unit, Proposition
4.4
yields

\displaystyle\operatorname{length}_{\mathcal{O}}\Psi_{R_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},\infty}}(M_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},\infty})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{length}_{\mathcal{O}}\Psi_{R_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},\infty}}(M_{\Sigma,\infty})+\nu_{\mathcal{O}}(\lambda(u_{v})^{\mu}\lambda(B_{v})^{\mu})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{length}_{\mathcal{O}}\Psi_{R_{\Sigma,\infty}}(M_{\Sigma,\infty})+\mu\cdot\nu_{\mathcal{O}}(\lambda(B_{v})).

We note that the hypotheses of Proposition
4.4
are satisfied as Lemma
(Wt1)
yields that
M_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},\infty}
and
M_{\Sigma,\infty}
have the same rank
\mu
at
\lambda
and, as noted after Lemma
(Wt1)
, we know that both these modules are maximal Cohen-Macaulay over
R_{\Sigma\cup\{v\},\infty}
and self-dual.

Since
R_{\varnothing,\infty}
is formally smooth and hence regular, we get that
\Phi_{R_{\varnothing,\infty}}=0
and

\Psi_{R_{\varnothing,\infty}}(M_{\varnothing,\infty})=\Psi_{R_{\varnothing,\infty}}(R_{\varnothing,\infty}^{\mu})=\Psi_{R_{\varnothing,\infty}}(R_{\varnothing,\infty})^{\mu}=\Phi_{R_{\varnothing,\infty}}^{\mu}=0\,.

by Theorem
9.5
. It now follows by induction that

\operatorname{length}_{\mathcal{O}}\Psi_{R_{\Sigma,\infty}}(M_{\Sigma,\infty})=\mu\sum_{v\in\Sigma}\nu_{\mathcal{O}}(\lambda(B_{v}))

for any
\Sigma\subseteq T
.

On the other hand, by §
12.2
and Proposition
(Wt1)
, one gets that
R_{\Sigma,\infty}
is a power series ring over

\displaystyle\widehat{\bigotimes_{{v\in\Sigma}}}R_{v}^{{\rm uni}(\epsilon_{v})}
. By Proposition
(Wt1)
it is thus a power series ring over

\widehat{\bigotimes_{v\in\Sigma}}\mathcal{O}[\![{A_{v},B_{v}}]\!]/(A_{v}B_{v})

An easy computation now gives that

\Phi_{R_{\Sigma,\infty}}\cong\prod_{v\in\Sigma}\left(\mathcal{O}/\lambda(B_{v})\mathcal{O}\right)

and so

\operatorname{length}_{\mathcal{O}}\Phi_{R_{\Sigma,\infty}}=\sum_{v\in\Sigma}\nu_{\mathcal{O}}(\lambda(B_{v})).

Hence
\delta_{R_{\Sigma,\infty}}(M_{\Sigma,\infty})=0
for all
\Sigma\subseteq T
.

As each
R_{\Sigma,\infty}
is a complete intersection (and hence Gorenstein), Theorem
(Wt1)
now follows from Theorems
9.2
and
9.5
.
∎

Glossary

-algebras,

,

-module

,

(A,\lambda)

such that
A
is regular at
\operatorname{Ker}(\lambda)
,

pairs
(A,\lambda)
in
\operatorname{C}_{\mathcal{O}}
with

,

,

-module
M
,

-module
M
,

\operatorname{mod}A
,

a category of

-complexes with derived
